   26: # Scalar-binding operators: the value is always passed as a bind param (?), so
   27: # no quoting or escaping is required on the value side.  LIKE and NOT LIKE are
   28: # safe for the same reason â€” the pattern is bound, not interpolated.
   29: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_SQL_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('>', '<', '>=', '<=', '!=', '=', 'LIKE', 'NOT LIKE');
   30: 
   31: # List-membership operators whose criterion value is an arrayref.
   32: # Each element is bound as a separate ?, so injection is impossible.
   33: # IN  with an empty list â†’ WHERE 1=0 (no rows match, correct SQL semantics).
   34: # NOT IN with an empty list â†’ no WHERE term (all rows match, correct semantics).
   35: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_LIST_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IN', 'NOT IN');
   36: 
   37: # Nullability operators: no bind parameter.  The hashref value is ignored â€”
   38: # any value (undef, 1, ...) signals intent; only the key selects the operator.
   39: # A bare undef criterion value (col => undef) also generates IS NULL.
   40: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_NOARG_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IS NULL', 'IS NOT NULL');

  106: 
  107: =head1 NAME
  108: 
  109: Database::Join - Read-only combined view across two or more Database::Abstraction objects
  110: 
  111: =head1 VERSION
  112: 
  113: Version 0.010.0
  114: 
  115: =head1 SYNOPSIS
  116: 
  117: B<Basic two-database join>
  118: 
  119:     use Database::Join;
  120: 
  121:     # Step 1: create each component database the normal way
  122:     my $customers = Database::Customers->new(directory => '/data');
  123:     my $loyalty   = Database::Loyalty->new(directory  => '/data');
  124: 
  125:     # Step 2: combine them on the shared key column 'entry'
  126:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  127:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  128:         join_column => 'entry',
  129:     );
  130: 
  131:     # Step 3: query exactly as you would a single Database::Abstraction object
  132:     my $all_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  133:     my $vip_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
  134:     my $one_row   = $join->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'C001');
  135:     my $total     = $join->count();
  136:     my $col_names = $join->columns();
  137: 
  138: B<Hiding internal columns>
  139: 
  140:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  141:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  142:         join_column    => 'entry',
  143:         remove_columns => [ 'internal_id', 'audit_ts' ],
  144:     );
  145:     # 'internal_id' and 'audit_ts' never appear in results or columns()
  146: 
  147: B<join_map: when the key column has different names in each database>
  148: 
  149:     # $cities  (index 0) has a column called 'statecode' -- matches join_column
  150:     # $stnames (index 1) has a column called 'entry'     -- different name
  151: 
  152:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  153:         databases   => [ $cities,  $stnames ],
  154:         #                index 0   index 1
  155:         join_column => 'statecode',
  156:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },  # index 1 calls its join key 'entry'
  157:     );
  158: 
  159:     # All returned rows use 'statecode'; 'entry' is never exposed
  160:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  161: 
  162: B<filters: permanently restrict a database's visible rows>
  163: 
  164:     # Only show orders placed more than 60 days ago, without repeating
  165:     # the criterion on every query call.
  166:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  167:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
  168:         join_column => 'entry',
  169:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
  170:     );
  171: 
  172:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();                 # all old orders
  173:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');   # old + gold tier
  174: 
  175: B<Inner and outer join types>
  176: 
  177:     my $inner = Database::Join->new(
  178:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  179:         join_column => 'entry',
  180:         join_type   => 'inner',   # only keys present in BOTH databases
  181:     );
  182: 
  183:     my $outer = Database::Join->new(
  184:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  185:         join_column => 'entry',
  186:         join_type   => 'outer',   # all keys from EITHER database
  187:     );
  188: 
  189: B<Building the view incrementally with add_database>
  190: 
  191:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  192:         databases   => [ $customers ],
  193:         join_column => 'entry',
  194:     );
  195: 
  196:     $join->add_database($loyalty)
  197:          ->add_database($scores, remove_columns => ['raw_score']);
  198: 
  199: B<AUTOLOAD column shortcut>
  200: 
  201:     # Returns the 'name' value for entry 'C001' (scalar context)
  202:     my $name = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
  203: 
  204:     # Returns all 'tier' values (list context)
  205:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
  206: 
  207: B<aggregate(): GROUP BY across the merged view>
  208: 
  209:     # Count and total score per tier, highest total first
  210:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
  211:         by       => ['tier'],
  212:         measures => [
  213:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
  214:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
  215:         ],
  216:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
  217:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
  218:     );
  219:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
  220: 
  221:     # Grand total across the whole view (no 'by' key)
  222:     my $summary = $join->aggregate(
  223:         measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }],
  224:     );
  225:     # => [ { n => 42 } ]
  226: 
  227: B<SQLite join backend for large datasets>
  228: 
  229:     # 'auto' (default): switches to SQLite automatically above the threshold
  230:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  231:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  232:         join_column    => 'entry',
  233:         backend        => 'auto',          # default
  234:         max_array_rows => 50_000,          # use SQLite when combined rows > 50,000
  235:     );
  236: 
  237:     # Always use SQLite -- useful when you know the data is large
  238:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  239:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  240:         join_column => 'entry',
  241:         backend     => 'sqlite',
  242:         tmpdir      => '/fast/nvme/tmp',   # optional: faster temp disk
  243:     );
  244: 
  245:     # Always use the original in-memory path
  246:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  247:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  248:         join_column => 'entry',
  249:         backend     => 'array',
  250:     );
  251: 
  252: =head1 DESCRIPTION
  253: 
  254: C<Database::Join> merges two or more L<Database::Abstraction> objects into a
  255: single logical, read-only view.  Each component database is queried
  256: independently through its own C<Database::Abstraction> interface.  The results
  257: are combined using a shared key column (C<join_column>).
  258: In effect, this means that you can view data from more than one database using
  259: an intuitive, non-SQL interface.
  260: 
  261: Every storage format that C<Database::Abstraction> supports works as a
  262: component database: CSV, PSV, TSV, SQLite, JSON, XML, XLSX, BerkeleyDB, HTML
  263: URL, JSON URL, or any custom subclass.  Component databases may mix formats
  264: within the same join.
  265: 
  266: The module exposes the same read-only API as C<Database::Abstraction>:
  267: C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>, C<count>,
  268: C<columns>, C<schema>, C<updated>, C<set_logger>, and the AUTOLOAD column
  269: shortcut.  Callers do not need to know how many underlying databases are
  270: involved.
  271: 
  272: Think of it as a virtual database table that is assembled on demand from
  273: several real tables, one per component database.
  274: 
  275: B<Join backends>
  276: 
  277: By default (C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>), C<Database::Join> first checks the
  278: combined source row count.  For small datasets (up to C<max_array_rows>,
  279: default 10,000 rows) it merges entirely in Perl memory.  For larger datasets it
  280: automatically spills source rows into a temporary SQLite database and executes
  281: a single SQL JOIN there, keeping peak RAM to roughly one times the source data
  282: size instead of three.  You can also force either path unconditionally with
  283: C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> or C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>.
  284: 
  285: =head2 Join semantics
  286: 
  287: The C<join_type> parameter controls what happens when a particular key value
  288: exists in some component databases but not all:
  289: 
  290: =over 4
  291: 
  292: =item C<left> (the default)
  293: 
  294: All rows from the I<primary> (first) database are returned.  Columns from
  295: subsequent databases are included where a matching row is found, and simply
  296: absent from the hashref where there is no match.  If you are familiar with
  297: SQL, this is a LEFT OUTER JOIN on the first table.
  298: 
  299: =item C<inner>
  300: 
  301: Only rows whose join-column value is present in I<every> component database
  302: are returned.  This is equivalent to a SQL INNER JOIN.
  303: 
  304: =item C<outer>
  305: 
  306: Every join-column value found in I<any> component database is returned.
  307: Columns from databases that do not have that key value are absent from the
  308: merged row.  This is a FULL OUTER JOIN.
  309: 
  310: =back
  311: 
  312: B<Important override rule:> whenever you pass a query criterion for a column
  313: that belongs to a secondary database, that database automatically acts as an
  314: inner-join partner for that query only -- regardless of C<join_type>.  This
  315: gives WHERE-clause semantics.  For example, if you have a LEFT join but query
  316: C<< tier => 'gold' >> on a secondary database, only rows whose secondary entry
  317: has tier = 'gold' are returned (rows with no secondary entry are excluded, just
  318: as a WHERE clause would exclude them).
  319: 
  320: =head2 Column ownership and routing
  321: 
  322: At construction time, C<Database::Join> calls C<columns()> on each component
  323: database and builds an internal index that maps every column name to the
  324: database that owns it.
  325: 
  326: When you pass criteria to a query method, each key-value pair is automatically
  327: routed to the right database.  You never need to say which database a column
  328: belongs to.
  329: 
  330: The C<join_column> is special: criteria on it are broadcast to I<all>
  331: databases so that each database fetches only the relevant rows before the
  332: in-memory merge.
  333: 
  334: When the same non-join column name exists in more than one database, the
  335: I<last> database in the C<databases> array wins by default: its value
  336: overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.  Use C<collision_prefix> to
  337: preserve both values under distinct names instead.
  338: 
  339: =head1 LIMITATIONS
  340: 
  341: =over 4
  342: 
  343: =item Memory usage (array backend)
  344: 
  345: When C<backend> is C<'array'> (or C<'auto'> and the dataset is small), all
  346: matching rows are fetched into Perl memory.  Peak RAM is roughly three times
  347: the source data size.  For large datasets use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>, or
  348: leave C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> and set C<max_array_rows> appropriately.
  349: 
  350: =item SQLite backend uses a persistent cache file
  351: 
  352: When the SQLite path is active, a single C<.db> file with a randomly generated
  353: name (chosen by C<File::Temp> to avoid collisions) is created in C<tmpdir> the
  354: first time a query runs on a given C<Database::Join> object.  Source data is
  355: spilled into that file once; subsequent queries against the same object reuse
  356: the file without re-fetching the source data.
  357: 
  358: The cache is automatically invalidated and rebuilt whenever any source
  359: database's C<updated()> timestamp changes (indicating new data), or when
  360: C<add_database()> is called.
  361: 
  362: The file is deleted when the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (typically
  363: when it goes out of scope).  At any given moment no more than one such file
  364: exists per object.  The directory must be writable and have enough free space
  365: for the full source data (once, not per-query).
  366: 
  367: =item No chained builder or raw SQL
  368: 
  369: C<query()> and C<execute()> are not implemented.  Use C<selectall_arrayref>
  370: or C<fetchrow_hashref> instead.
  371: 
  372: =item Single-column equi-join only
  373: 
  374: Joining on more than one column simultaneously, or on expressions, is not
  375: supported.  When the join key has different names in different databases,
  376: use C<join_map> to declare each database's local column name.
  377: 
  378: =item Sort order
  379: 
  380: Results are sorted ascending by C<join_column> by default.  Pass
  381: C<< sort_by => 'colname' >> (or C<< sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC'] >>)
  382: to any query method to override this.  The array path uses string comparison
  383: (C<cmp>); for accurate numeric ordering on large datasets use the SQLite
  384: backend, which sorts natively by type.
  385: 
  386: =item count() on the array backend fetches all rows
  387: 
  388: On the array backend, C<count()> executes the full in-memory join and counts
  389: the resulting rows in Perl; no C<COUNT(*)> is pushed to the component
  390: databases.  On the SQLite backend, C<count()> executes a C<SELECT COUNT(*)>
  391: SQL query against the cached join tables, avoiding a full row transfer.
  392: 
  393: =back
  394: 
  395: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
  396: 
  397: =over 4
  398: 
  399: =item The join_column must exist in every component database
  400: 
  401: If even one database is missing the join key column, C<new()> (or
  402: C<add_database()>) will C<croak> immediately.  Use C<join_map> when the
  403: column has a different local name in some databases.
  404: 
  405: =item Criteria on a removed column are silently dropped
  406: 
  407: If you call C<remove_column('tier')> and later query
  408: C<< selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold') >>, the criterion is ignored (with a
  409: C<carp> warning) and all rows are returned.  Always pass criteria before
  410: removing columns, or restructure your code to avoid this.
  411: 
  412: =item You cannot remove the join column
  413: 
  414: C<< $join->remove_column($join->join_column) >> will C<croak>.  The join key
  415: is required for the merge to work.
  416: 
  417: =item Left join does not guarantee all columns are populated
  418: 
  419: Under a LEFT join, rows from the primary database that have no matching row
  420: in a secondary database will be returned with I<no keys> from that secondary
  421: database.  Accessing C<< $row->{score} >> on such a row returns C<undef> --
  422: not zero, not an empty string.  Always test C<defined $row->{score}> rather
  423: than just C<$row->{score}> when the secondary match is optional.
  424: 
  425: =item Filters act as inner-join partners
  426: 
  427: Any database that has a C<filters> entry is promoted to an inner-join partner,
  428: regardless of C<join_type>.  A row whose join-key value does not appear in
  429: the filtered database's result is removed from the merged output entirely, not
  430: merely missing its secondary columns.  This is intentional but can be
  431: surprising if you expected LEFT join semantics.
  432: 
  433: =item Criteria-merging replaces scalar filters
  434: 
  435: When both a base filter and a query criterion target the same column, and both
  436: are operator hashrefs (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >>), the operators are combined
  437: (AND semantics).  But if the query criterion is a plain scalar (e.g.
  438: C<< score => 75 >>), it I<replaces> the base filter for that column entirely --
  439: the base filter is ignored for that query.
  440: 
  441: =item AUTOLOAD sees the full merged join when filters or join_map are active
  442: 
  443: When either C<filters> or C<join_map> is in effect, the AUTOLOAD shortcut
  444: (C<< $join->columnname(...) >>) runs the full join query rather than
  445: delegating directly to the owning database.  This is necessary for correctness
  446: but means the result respects all active filters and join-key translations,
  447: which may differ from what the owning database would return on its own.
  448: 
  449: =item Duplicate column names: last database wins (unless collision_prefix is set)
  450: 
  451: When two component databases each have a column called C<notes>, the second
  452: database's value silently overwrites the first in every merged row.  Use
  453: C<collision_prefix => { 1 => 'right' }> to publish the second database's
  454: C<notes> as C<right.notes> so both values survive, or use C<remove_columns>
  455: (or C<remove_column>) to drop the unwanted duplicate entirely.
  456: 
  457: =item Mutating the filters hashref after construction has no effect
  458: 
  459: C<Database::Join> deep-copies the C<filters> hashref (and any C<filter>
  460: passed to C<add_database>) at the moment of construction.  The original hashref
  461: you passed in is never stored.  If you later modify it -- for example, to
  462: tighten or loosen a filter criterion -- the joined view is I<not> affected.
  463: Construct a new C<Database::Join> object, or use a component
  464: C<Database::Abstraction> that supports dynamic filter modification.
  465: 
  466: =item auto mode may always use the array path for some DAs
  467: 
  468: C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> counts rows cheaply only when each component database
  469: either implements C<dbi_source()> (SQLite-backed) or directly defines a
  470: C<count()> method in its own package.  A DA that merely I<inherits> C<count()>
  471: from C<Database::Abstraction> is treated as uncountable, because the parent
  472: class C<count()> expects a key argument and behaves differently from a
  473: "return total row count" function.  In that case C<Database::Join>
  474: conservatively uses the array path for the whole query, even if the dataset is
  475: large.  To opt in to the SQLite path for such a DA, either add your own
  476: C<count()> override that returns the total row count, implement C<dbi_source()>,
  477: or use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> unconditionally.
  478: 
  479: =item dbi_source() ATTACH is unconditional - query-time criteria go into WHERE
  480: 
  481: When a component database implements C<dbi_source()>, C<Database::Join> always
  482: uses the zero-copy ATTACH path, I<even when the current query includes criteria
  483: for columns in that database>.  The criteria are translated into parameterised
  484: SQL C<WHERE> clauses applied against the ATTACHed table; no row-level copy is
  485: performed.  (Prior to 0.005.0 the presence of any query-time criteria would
  486: force a spill; that restriction has been removed.)
  487: 
  488: =item Broadcast join-column criterion does not force secondaries into inner-join
  489: 
  490: When a caller passes a join-column criterion (e.g. C<entry =E<gt> 'k1'>),
  491: C<Database::Join> broadcasts it to all component databases so each DA can
  492: filter its fetch to the requested key.  Prior to 0.006.0 this broadcast was
  493: incorrectly counted as "having criteria" for secondary databases, causing
  494: C<left> and C<outer> joins to silently behave as C<inner> joins when a
  495: join-column criterion was present.  The fix: only non-join-column criteria
  496: (e.g. column filters from the caller or base C<filters =E<gt> {...}>) promote
  497: a secondary to inner-join status.  The broadcast itself is now a transparent
  498: key-range selector that does not affect join semantics.
  499: 
  500: =item LIKE and NOT LIKE work on the SQLite path; other pattern operators do not yet
  501: 
  502: C<LIKE>, C<NOT LIKE>, C<IN>, and C<NOT IN> are fully supported on the SQLite
  503: backend.  C<LIKE>/C<NOT LIKE> take a scalar pattern; C<IN>/C<NOT IN> take an
  504: arrayref of values.  All are injection-safe because values are passed as bind
  505: parameters, never interpolated.
  506: 
  507:     # LIKE
  508:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(name => { LIKE => 'A%' });
  509: 
  510:     # IN
  511:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { IN => ['gold', 'silver'] });
  512: 
  513:     # NOT IN
  514:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { 'NOT IN' => ['bronze'] });
  515: 
  516: C<IN> with an empty arrayref matches no rows (SQL semantics: C<IN ()> is
  517: always false).  C<NOT IN> with an empty arrayref matches all rows (no
  518: constraint added).
  519: 
  520: C<IS NULL> and C<IS NOT NULL> are supported on the SQLite backend using an
  521: explicit operator hashref:
  522: 
  523:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NULL'     => undef });
  524:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NOT NULL' => 1    });
  525: 
  526: The hashref value is ignored; only the key selects the operator.  A bare
  527: C<undef> criterion value (C<< score => undef >>) also generates C<IS NULL>
  528: on the SQLite path.  Note: on the in-memory array path, the component DA
  529: may treat an C<undef> criterion value as C<< no filter >> rather than
  530: C<IS NULL>, so use the explicit hashref form for consistent behaviour
  531: across backends.
  532: 
  533: Any operator not in the supported set is skipped on the SQLite path with a
  534: C<carp> warning.  The array path forwards all operators to the component DA
  535: unchanged, which may or may not honour them.
  536: 
  537: =item Temp file directory must be writable and have free space
  538: 
  539: The SQLite path creates one temporary C<.db> file per C<Database::Join> object
  540: in C<tmpdir> (default: C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()>, usually C</tmp> on Unix).
  541: The filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp> -- you cannot predict it,
  542: only the directory is under your control.  The file is created on the first
  543: query and kept alive until the object is destroyed; it is not re-created on
  544: every query call.  If the directory is not writable, or the filesystem is full,
  545: the call will C<croak> with C<error_sqlite_connect>.  Check permissions and
  546: free space if you see that error.
  547: 
  548: =item C<join =E<gt>> criteria are not supported
  549: 
  550: C<Database::Abstraction> accepts a C<join =E<gt> { table =E<gt> ..., on =E<gt> ... }>
  551: key in its criteria hashrefs to express an SQL JOIN within a single table.
  552: C<Database::Join> cannot route this to a component DA meaningfully: the merged
  553: view has no concept of a single underlying table.  Passing C<join =E<gt>> to any
  554: query method will C<croak> with C<error_join_criterion>.
  555: 
  556: B<Fix:> Model the joined table as a separate C<Database::Abstraction> object and
  557: add it to the C<databases =E<gt> []> list.  Use C<join_column> or C<join_map> to
  558: specify the shared key.
  559: 
  560: =item aggregate(): omitting C<by> gives a grand total, not an error
  561: 
  562: Calling C<aggregate()> without a C<by> key (or with C<< by => [] >>) does
  563: I<not> croak.  It returns a single-element arrayref containing one row with
  564: all the computed measure aliases.  This is a grand total over the entire view
  565: (possibly filtered by any extra criteria you pass).
  566: 
  567:     my $total = $join->aggregate(measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }]);
  568:     # $total->[0]{n} is the total row count
  569: 
  570: If you expected multiple rows and got one, you probably forgot the C<by> key.
  571: 
  572: =item aggregate(): non-numeric values behave differently across backends
  573: 
  574: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), any non-numeric value in a
  575: column targeted by C<sum> or C<avg> is I<skipped> (not counted, not coerced),
  576: and a C<carp> warning C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric> is emitted.  On the SQLite
  577: backend, non-numeric values are silently coerced to C<0.0> via SQL C<CAST>.
  578: The two backends therefore produce different numeric totals when the data
  579: contains non-numeric values.  Clean your data first if you need consistent
  580: results across backends, or choose a backend explicitly.
  581: 
  582: =item aggregate(): C<BETWEEN> is not supported in C<having>
  583: 
  584: The C<having> hashref uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>,
  585: which does not include C<BETWEEN>.  Write a range as two explicit constraints:
  586: 
  587:     # Correct
  588:     having => { total => { '>=' => 10, '<=' => 50 } }
  589: 
  590:     # Wrong -- will croak with error_aggregate_having_operator
  591:     having => { total => { BETWEEN => [10, 50] } }
  592: 
  593: =item aggregate(): C<count> without C<col> counts all rows, not distinct non-NULL values
  594: 
  595: A measure with C<< fn => 'count' >> and no C<col> key emits C<COUNT(*)> on the
  596: SQLite backend (or counts every row in Perl).  This counts rows that have
  597: C<undef>/NULL in other columns too.  To count only rows where a specific
  598: column is non-NULL, or to count distinct values, use C<count_distinct>:
  599: 
  600:     # Count rows with a non-NULL 'score' (one way -- use a pre-filter)
  601:     measures => [{ col => 'score', fn => 'count_distinct', as => 'distinct_scores' }]
  602: 
  603: =item aggregate(): the result is never cached
  604: 
  605: Unlike C<selectall_arrayref> (which benefits from the SQLite file cache on
  606: repeated identical calls), C<aggregate()> always re-executes.  If your
  607: aggregation is expensive and the data changes infrequently, cache the result
  608: yourself using C<updated()> as a staleness signal.
  609: 
  610: =back
  611: 
  612: =head1 METHODS
  613: 
  614: =head2 new
  615: 
  616: =head3 SYNOPSIS
  617: 
  618:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  619:         databases        => [ $db1, $db2 ],
  620:         join_column      => 'entry',
  621:         join_type        => 'left',
  622:         join_map         => { 1 => 'local_col' },
  623:         filters          => { 1 => { score => { '>' => 60 } } },
  624:         collision_prefix => { 1 => 'right' },
  625:         remove_columns   => [ 'email', 'internal_id' ],
  626:         backend          => 'auto',        # 'auto' | 'sqlite' | 'array'
  627:         max_array_rows   => 10_000,        # threshold for 'auto' mode
  628:         tmpdir           => '/tmp',        # directory for temp SQLite file
  629:         logger           => $log,
  630:         i18n             => $locale,
  631:     );
  632: 
  633: =head3 DESCRIPTION
  634: 
  635: Constructs and returns a new C<Database::Join> object.
  636: 
  637: Each element of C<databases> must be an already-instantiated subclass of
  638: C<Database::Abstraction>.  The constructor calls C<columns()> on every
  639: database to build an internal column-routing table and verifies that
  640: C<join_column> (or its local alias from C<join_map>) is present in each one.
  641: 
  642: Columns listed in C<remove_columns> are hidden immediately: they do not appear
  643: in C<columns()>, C<schema()>, or any returned row hashref.  This is equivalent
  644: to calling C<remove_column> once per name after construction.
  645: 
  646: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
  647: 
  648: =head4 Input
  649: 
  650:     databases      => { type => 'arrayref', required => 1 }
  651:                       # One or more Database::Abstraction subclass objects.
  652:                       #
  653:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   non-empty arrayref of blessed DA subclasses.
  654:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: scalar, hashref, or absent => croak.
  655:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA size:   minimum 1 element; no documented upper bound.
  656:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA elem:   each element must pass isa('Database::Abstraction').
  657: 
  658:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'entry' }
  659:                       # The column name shared by all databases (the join key).
  660:                       #
  661:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-empty string present in every component DA.
  662:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: column absent from any DA => croak join_col_missing.
  663:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        empty string '' is treated as a column name and
  664:                       #                       will croak if (as expected) it is absent from every DA.
  665:                       # DOMAIN -- NOTE:       matching is case-sensitive and exact.
  666: 
  667:     join_type      => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'left',
  668:                         enum => ['inner', 'left', 'outer'] }
  669:                       # Controls which keys appear in the result when not all
  670:                       # databases share the same key values.
  671:                       #
  672:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   exactly 'inner', 'left', or 'outer'.
  673:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string including 'INNER', 'LEFT',
  674:                       #                       'OUTER' (enum check is case-sensitive), 'cross',
  675:                       #                       or '' => croak from validate_strict.
  676: 
  677:     join_map       => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  678:                       # Zero-based database index => local column name.
  679:                       # See the join_map section for full details.
  680:                       #
  681:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref values must be plain strings.
  682:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: reference value (hashref, arrayref, coderef, etc.)
  683:                       #                       => croak; the guard prevents heap-address leakage.
  684:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        out-of-range keys (beyond the databases array) are
  685:                       #                       silently ignored.
  686: 
  687:     filters        => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  688:                       # Zero-based database index => criteria hashref.
  689:                       # Permanent row restrictions on individual databases.
  690:                       # See the filters section for full details.
  691: 
  692:     base_criteria  => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  693:                       # Column name => value hashref.
  694:                       # Permanent criteria applied to every query, specified by
  695:                       # column name rather than database index.  Each key must
  696:                       # be a column that appears in the merged view (or the
  697:                       # join_column); each value is a plain scalar or an
  698:                       # operator hashref in the same format as
  699:                       # selectall_arrayref accepts.  Criteria are automatically
  700:                       # routed to the database that owns each column (same
  701:                       # routing used by selectall_arrayref).
  702:                       # Equivalent to filters but more convenient when you know
  703:                       # the column name but not which database index owns it.
  704:                       # When a column appears in both base_criteria and filters,
  705:                       # the filters entry takes precedence.
  706:                       # See the base_criteria section for full details.
  707:                       #
  708:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of column-name => scalar or
  709:                       #                       operator-hashref pairs.  Unknown column
  710:                       #                       names emit warn_unknown_column (carp)
  711:                       #                       and are silently dropped.
  712:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-hashref value => croak from
  713:                       #                       validate_strict.
  714:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        {} empty hashref is a safe no-op.
  715: 
  716:     collision_prefix => { type => 'hashref', optional => 1 }
  717:                       # Zero-based database index (>0) => prefix string.
  718:                       # When a secondary database has a column that collides
  719:                       # with a column already present in the merged view, the
  720:                       # secondary column is published as "$prefix.$col" instead
  721:                       # of silently overwriting the earlier value.
  722:                       # Index 0 entries are silently ignored.
  723:                       # Omitting this parameter preserves the original
  724:                       # last-database-wins behaviour.
  725:                       # See the collision_prefix section for full details.
  726:                       #
  727:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   absent or {} => last-database-wins (no change).
  728:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   { N => 'prefix' } where N > 0 => colliding
  729:                       #                       columns from DB[N] published as "$prefix.$col";
  730:                       #                       non-colliding columns from the same DB added plain.
  731:                       # DOMAIN -- EP note:    index-0 entries are silently ignored.
  732:                       # DOMAIN -- Invariant:  join_column is never prefixed regardless of
  733:                       #                       collision_prefix configuration.
  734: 
  735:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
  736:                       # Column names to hide from the merged view.
  737:                       #
  738:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of any strings; non-existent columns
  739:                       #                       are silently ignored (idempotent).
  740:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak remove_join_col.
  741:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        [] empty arrayref is a safe no-op.
  742: 
  743:     backend        => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'auto',
  744:                         enum => ['array', 'sqlite', 'auto'] }
  745:                       # Controls which join strategy is used.
  746:                       #   'auto'   -- (default) use 'array' when combined source row count
  747:                       #              <= max_array_rows, 'sqlite' otherwise.
  748:                       #   'sqlite' -- always spill to a temporary SQLite database.
  749:                       #   'array'  -- always use the in-memory merge path.
  750:                       #
  751:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto' (case-sensitive).
  752:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string => croak error_invalid_backend.
  753:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    'auto'.
  754: 
  755:     max_array_rows => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 10_000 }
  756:                       # Row-count threshold for 'auto' mode.  When the combined
  757:                       # source row count exceeds this value, the SQLite path is used.
  758:                       # Ignored when backend is 'array' or 'sqlite'.
  759:                       #
  760:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-negative integer.
  761:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        0 means always use SQLite (all counts exceed 0).
  762:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    10,000.
  763: 
  764:     tmpdir         => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
  765:                       # Directory for the per-call temporary SQLite database file.
  766:                       # The file is created securely by File::Temp and removed when
  767:                       # the query completes.  Ignored when backend is 'array'.
  768:                       #
  769:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any writable directory path string.
  770:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  uses File::Spec->tmpdir() (system temp dir).
  771: 
  772:     parallel       => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 0 }
  773:                       # When set to 1 and the join has more than 2 databases (primary +
  774:                       # 2 or more secondaries), secondary DA fetches are issued in
  775:                       # parallel Perl threads.  Requires the 'threads' module; falls
  776:                       # back to sequential with a carp warning when unavailable.
  777:                       # Has no effect on the SQLite backend (which uses a single SQL
  778:                       # JOIN).  DBI-backed DAs are not thread-safe by default; only
  779:                       # enable this for in-memory or otherwise thread-safe DA backends.
  780:                       #
  781:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   0 (sequential, default) or 1 (parallel).
  782:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other integer is treated as truthy/falsy.
  783:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    0.
  784: 
  785:     logger         => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  786:                       # Logger object propagated to all component databases.
  787: 
  788:     i18n           => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  789:                       # Localisation object with a translate($key, @args) method.
  790: 
  791: =head4 Output
  792: 
  793:     A blessed Database::Join object.
  794: 
  795: =head3 EXAMPLE
  796: 
  797:     # Customers database: entry | name | email
  798:     # Loyalty   database: entry | tier | points
  799: 
  800:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  801:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  802:         join_column    => 'entry',
  803:         join_type      => 'inner',            # only customers who also have loyalty records
  804:         remove_columns => [ 'email' ],        # hide PII from query results
  805:         filters        => { 1 => { points => { '>' => 0 } } }, # ignore zero-point records
  806:     );
  807: 
  808:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  809:     # Each row: { entry => ..., name => ..., tier => ..., points => ... }
  810:     # 'email' is absent. Zero-point loyalty records are excluded.
  811: 
  812: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
  813: 
  814:     validate all parameters with validate_strict
  815:     croak if databases is empty
  816:     croak if any element of databases is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
  817:     bless the object with all fields initialised
  818:     call _build_col_index to map every column to its owning database
  819:         and verify join_column presence in each database
  820:     if base_criteria given:
  821:         partition base_criteria by column ownership into per-db slices
  822:         for each database with a non-empty slice:
  823:             merge the slice into _filters[i]
  824:             (explicit filters win on plain-scalar conflicts)
  825:     for each column in remove_columns: call remove_column
  826:     return the new object
  827: 
  828: =head3 MESSAGES
  829: 
  830:     error_no_databases        -- databases arrayref was empty
  831:     error_invalid_db          -- an element of databases is not a D::A subclass
  832:     error_join_col_missing    -- join_column (or its join_map alias) not found in a database
  833:     error_invalid_backend     -- backend value is not 'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto'
  834:     error_sqlite_connect      -- temporary SQLite database could not be created (backend='sqlite'/'auto')
  835:     warn_schema_type_mismatch -- (carp) a shared column has different types across databases;
  836:                                  use collision_prefix to preserve both values
  837: 
  838: =cut
  839:

  926: 		_max_array_rows   => $p->{max_array_rows},
  927: 		_tmpdir           => $p->{tmpdir} // File::Spec->tmpdir,

  929: 		_mandatory_filters => [],   # validated and replaced below after _build_col_index

  989: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  990: # Public API (mirrors Database::Abstraction)
  991: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  992: 
  993: =head2 join_map - joining on differently-named columns
  994: 
  995: By default every component database must have a column whose name matches
  996: C<join_column>.  If a database uses a different local name for the join key,
  997: declare the mapping with C<join_map>.
  998: 
  999: C<join_map> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1000: database in the C<databases> array (0 = first, 1 = second, and so on).  Each
 1001: B<value> is the name that B<that particular database> uses for the join key.
 1002: 
 1003: Databases not listed in C<join_map> are assumed to already have a column
 1004: named C<join_column> and need no entry.
 1005: 
 1006: Throughout the merged view the join key is I<always> referred to by the name
 1007: given in C<join_column>.  The local alias is never exposed in returned rows,
 1008: in C<columns()>, or in C<schema()>.
 1009: 
 1010: B<When do you need join_map?>
 1011: 
 1012: You need C<join_map> when you have two tables like:
 1013: 
 1014:     cities table  : entry (the city name) | statecode
 1015:     stnames table : entry (the state code) | state
 1016: 
 1017: Here you want to join cities.statecode to stnames.entry.  You choose
 1018: C<< join_column => 'statecode' >> as the canonical name, but stnames calls
 1019: that same concept C<entry>, so you declare:
 1020: 
 1021:     join_map => { 1 => 'entry' }  # stnames (index 1) calls it 'entry'
 1022: 
 1023: B<Example>
 1024: 
 1025:     #                        index 0     index 1
 1026:     my @databases = (       $cities,    $stnames  );
 1027:     #  join key column:    'statecode'  'entry'
 1028:     #  join_column:        'statecode' (chosen canonical name)
 1029:     #  stnames differs, so declare the alias:
 1030: 
 1031:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1032:         databases   => \@databases,
 1033:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1034:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },
 1035:     );
 1036: 
 1037:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1038:     # Each $row has keys: entry (city), statecode, state
 1039:     # 'entry' from stnames is never exposed directly.
 1040: 
 1041:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(statecode => 'CA');
 1042: 
 1043: B<Using add_database instead>
 1044: 
 1045: If you build the join incrementally with C<add_database>, pass
 1046: C<join_column> directly to that call instead of using C<join_map>:
 1047: 
 1048:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1049:         databases   => [ $cities ],
 1050:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1051:     );
 1052:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'entry');
 1053: 
 1054: This is exactly equivalent to the C<join_map> form above.
 1055: 
 1056: =head2 filters - permanent per-database row filters
 1057: 
 1058: C<filters> lets you restrict a component database to a subset of its rows
 1059: permanently, without repeating the criterion on every query call.
 1060: 
 1061: Think of it as telling the join: "whenever you query this database, always
 1062: add these extra conditions".  Callers never need to specify the restriction
 1063: themselves and can never accidentally omit it.
 1064: 
 1065: C<filters> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1066: database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).  Each
 1067: B<value> is a criteria hashref in the same format as C<selectall_arrayref>
 1068: accepts.
 1069: 
 1070: B<Key-set semantics>
 1071: 
 1072: A filtered database always acts as an inner-join partner, regardless of the
 1073: C<join_type> setting.  Any join-key value that does not pass the filter is
 1074: excluded from the merged output entirely -- not just missing its secondary
 1075: columns.  This ensures the filter genuinely restricts the view rather than
 1076: simply hiding a few fields.
 1077: 
 1078: B<Criteria merging>
 1079: 
 1080: When a query call also passes a criterion for a column that already has a base
 1081: filter, the two constraints are combined:
 1082: 
 1083: =over 4
 1084: 
 1085: =item *
 1086: 
 1087: When both the base filter value and the query criterion are operator hashrefs
 1088: (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >> and C<< { '<' => 365 } >>), their operators are
 1089: merged: I<both> constraints apply simultaneously (AND semantics).
 1090: 
 1091: =item *
 1092: 
 1093: When either value is a plain scalar, or the operators conflict, the
 1094: query-time criterion wins and the base filter for that column is ignored for
 1095: that one call.
 1096: 
 1097: =back
 1098: 
 1099: B<Example -- only show orders placed more than 60 days ago>
 1100: 
 1101:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1102:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1103:         join_column => 'entry',
 1104:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
 1105:     );
 1106: 
 1107:     # Every query automatically sees only old orders
 1108:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1109: 
 1110:     # Additional criteria layer on top -- gold tier AND old order
 1111:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1112: 
 1113:     # Range intersection: age_days > 60 AND age_days < 365
 1114:     my $mid  = $join->selectall_arrayref(age_days => { '<' => 365 });
 1115: 
 1116: When using C<add_database>, pass C<filter> (singular) to set the base
 1117: criteria for the new database:
 1118: 
 1119:     $join->add_database($orders, filter => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } });
 1120: 
 1121: =head2 base_criteria - permanent view-level row filters by column name
 1122: 
 1123: C<base_criteria> is a convenience alternative to C<filters> for callers who
 1124: know the column names they want to restrict but prefer not to track database
 1125: indices.
 1126: 
 1127:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1128:         databases     => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1129:         join_column   => 'entry',
 1130:         base_criteria => { active => 1, deleted_at => undef },
 1131:     );
 1132: 
 1133:     # Every query automatically sees only active, non-deleted rows.
 1134:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1135: 
 1136: At construction time C<base_criteria> is partitioned by column ownership
 1137: using the same routing logic as C<selectall_arrayref>.  Each criterion is
 1138: sent to the database that owns that column and stored as a permanent base
 1139: filter (equivalent to the corresponding C<filters> entry).
 1140: 
 1141: B<Unknown columns> emit a C<warn_unknown_column> carp and are silently
 1142: dropped, just as they would be in a query call.
 1143: 
 1144: B<Key-set semantics> are identical to C<filters>: any database that receives
 1145: a C<base_criteria> slice acts as an inner-join partner regardless of
 1146: C<join_type>.
 1147: 
 1148: B<Precedence>: when a column appears in both C<base_criteria> and C<filters>,
 1149: the C<filters> entry wins on plain-scalar conflicts; operator-hashref values
 1150: are combined with AND semantics.
 1151: 
 1152: B<Use cases>
 1153: 
 1154: =over 4
 1155: 
 1156: =item *
 1157: 
 1158: Row-level security: C<< base_criteria => { tenant_id => $tid } >>
 1159: 
 1160: =item *
 1161: 
 1162: Soft-delete filtering: C<< base_criteria => { deleted_at => undef } >>
 1163: 
 1164: =item *
 1165: 
 1166: Status gates: C<< base_criteria => { active => 1 } >>
 1167: 
 1168: =back
 1169: 
 1170: =head2 collision_prefix - preserve colliding columns from secondary databases
 1171: 
 1172: By default, when a column name appears in more than one database the I<last>
 1173: database wins: its value silently overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.
 1174: This loses data and makes the origin invisible.
 1175: 
 1176: C<collision_prefix> changes this for secondary databases you designate.
 1177: When a secondary database at index N has a column that already exists in the
 1178: merged view, and C<collision_prefix-E<gt>{N}> is set, the colliding column is
 1179: published as C<"$prefix.$col"> instead of overwriting.  Both values are then
 1180: visible: the original column keeps its name (from the earlier database), and
 1181: the collision gets the prefixed name.
 1182: 
 1183: C<collision_prefix> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based index> of
 1184: a secondary database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).
 1185: Each B<value> is the prefix string to prepend.  An index-0 entry is
 1186: meaningless and silently ignored.  Omitting C<collision_prefix> entirely
 1187: preserves the previous last-wins behaviour and changes nothing.
 1188: 
 1189: Non-colliding columns from a secondary database are always added as-is with
 1190: no prefix, whether or not C<collision_prefix> is configured.
 1191: 
 1192: B<Example -- sales table and products table, both with a "product" column>
 1193: 
 1194:     # $sales    columns: id, product, amount, date
 1195:     # $products columns: sku, product, price, category
 1196:     # join on 'product' (left key) matched against 'sku' (right key via join_map)
 1197: 
 1198:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1199:         databases        => [$sales, $products],
 1200:         join_column      => 'product',
 1201:         join_map         => { 1 => 'sku' },
 1202:         collision_prefix => { 1 => 'products' },
 1203:     );
 1204: 
 1205:     $join->columns;
 1206:     # => ['amount', 'category', 'date', 'id', 'price', 'product', 'products.product']
 1207:     #                                                               ^-- prefixed collision
 1208: 
 1209:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(product => 'widget');
 1210:     # $row->{product}            -- value from $sales
 1211:     # $row->{'products.product'} -- value from $products (different row, same column name)
 1212:     # $row->{price}              -- from $products, no collision, kept as-is
 1213: 
 1214: B<Querying on a prefixed column>
 1215: 
 1216: Use the full published name as the criterion key:
 1217: 
 1218:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('products.product' => 'widget');
 1219:     # Internally routes as: product => 'widget' to $products
 1220: 
 1221: B<Interaction with remove_column>
 1222: 
 1223: C<remove_column> operates on published names.  To suppress a prefixed
 1224: collision column entirely, pass the prefixed name:
 1225: 
 1226:     $join->remove_column('products.product');
 1227: 
 1228: =head2 backend - SQLite join backend for large datasets
 1229: 
 1230: C<Database::Join> can merge component databases in two different ways,
 1231: controlled by the C<backend> constructor parameter.
 1232: 
 1233: =over 4
 1234: 
 1235: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'array'> -- in-memory merge (original behaviour)
 1236: 
 1237: All matching rows are fetched from every component database into Perl hashes
 1238: and merged there.  Simple and fast for small and medium datasets.  Peak RAM
 1239: is roughly three times the combined source data size (one copy per database
 1240: plus one merged copy).
 1241: 
 1242: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> -- SQL JOIN via a cached temporary file
 1243: 
 1244: C<Database::Join> creates a temporary SQLite database file, spills source
 1245: rows into it (one table per component database), then executes a single SQL
 1246: C<JOIN> statement per query call.  Peak RAM drops to roughly one times the
 1247: source data size.
 1248: 
 1249: The temporary file is created once and reused across multiple query calls on
 1250: the same object (the cache).  Only query-time criteria vary per call; they
 1251: are applied as SQL C<WHERE> clauses against the cached data.  The cache is
 1252: automatically rebuilt when any source database's C<updated()> timestamp
 1253: changes.  The file is deleted when the object is destroyed (goes out of
 1254: scope).  See I<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime> below for
 1255: details.
 1256: 
 1257: Requires C<DBD::SQLite E<gt>= 1.70> (C<FULL OUTER JOIN> support was added in
 1258: SQLite 3.39.0; DBD::SQLite 1.70 ships SQLite 3.39.2).
 1259: 
 1260: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> (default)
 1261: 
 1262: C<Database::Join> counts the total rows from all component databases cheaply
 1263: -- without fetching them -- and then decides:
 1264: 
 1265: =over 4
 1266: 
 1267: =item *
 1268: 
 1269: If the combined count is less than or equal to C<max_array_rows> (default
 1270: 10,000), use the array path.
 1271: 
 1272: =item *
 1273: 
 1274: If the combined count exceeds C<max_array_rows>, use the SQLite path.
 1275: 
 1276: =back
 1277: 
 1278: For counting to work without fetching, each component database must either
 1279: implement the C<dbi_source()> interface (for SQLite-backed sources, where a
 1280: C<COUNT(*)> SQL query is issued directly), or directly define a C<count()>
 1281: method in its own package -- not just inherit one from a parent class.  If
 1282: neither is available for a particular database, C<Database::Join> plays it
 1283: safe and uses the array path for the whole query without fetching any rows.
 1284: 
 1285: =back
 1286: 
 1287: B<Choosing max_array_rows>
 1288: 
 1289: The default of 10,000 is a reasonable starting point.  Adjust it to match
 1290: your hardware and typical row width.  For wide rows (many columns or long
 1291: strings) you may want a lower threshold; for narrow rows you can raise it.
 1292: 
 1293: B<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime>
 1294: 
 1295: When the SQLite path is active, a single temporary SQLite database file acts
 1296: as the join cache for the life of the C<Database::Join> object.
 1297: 
 1298: B<Name>: the filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp>, for example:
 1299: 
 1300:     /tmp/Cj8xK7mP2Q.db
 1301: 
 1302: The random portion (ten characters) is chosen automatically to avoid
 1303: collisions.  Only the directory is under your control; you cannot specify
 1304: the filename itself.
 1305: 
 1306: B<Location>: controlled by the C<tmpdir> constructor parameter.
 1307: If C<tmpdir> is not specified, C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()> is used (usually
 1308: C</tmp> on Unix, or the value of the C<TEMP> or C<TMP> environment variable
 1309: on Windows).
 1310: 
 1311: B<Lifetime: one file per object, deleted when the object is destroyed>: the
 1312: file is created on the first query call that uses the SQLite path and kept
 1313: alive until the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (i.e. when it goes out
 1314: of scope or is explicitly C<undef>-d).  At most one file exists per object at
 1315: any given moment.  Calling C<selectall_arrayref()> ten times on the same
 1316: object creates and uses I<one> file, not ten.
 1317: 
 1318: B<Cache invalidation>: the cache is automatically rebuilt (the old file is
 1319: replaced with a new one) when any source database's C<updated()> return value
 1320: changes, or when C<add_database()> is called.  Base-filter criteria
 1321: (C<filters> constructor parameter) are applied once at build time for spilled
 1322: sources; query-time criteria are applied per-call as SQL C<WHERE> clauses.
 1323: 
 1324: To use a different directory -- for example a RAM-backed filesystem or a
 1325: faster local disk:
 1326: 
 1327:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1328:         databases      => [ $db1, $db2 ],
 1329:         join_column    => 'entry',
 1330:         backend        => 'sqlite',
 1331:         tmpdir         => '/dev/shm',     # Linux RAM disk
 1332:     );
 1333: 
 1334: B<Parallel secondary fetches (C<parallel> constructor parameter)>
 1335: 
 1336: By default, component databases are queried sequentially - the primary first,
 1337: then each secondary in order.  When the component databases are network- or
 1338: disk-backed and have non-trivial per-query latency, the sequential fetch means
 1339: total latency is the I<sum> of all per-DA latencies.
 1340: 
 1341: Setting C<< parallel => 1 >> in the constructor enables concurrent fetching
 1342: of secondary databases using Perl C<threads>.  With C<parallel => 1> and
 1343: two or more secondary databases (C<n E<gt> 2> total), secondary fetches run in
 1344: parallel after the primary fetch completes; total latency drops to
 1345: I<max(secondary latencies)> instead of I<sum(secondary latencies)>.
 1346: 
 1347:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1348:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty, $scores ],   # 3 DAs - 2 secondaries
 1349:         join_column => 'entry',
 1350:         parallel    => 1,    # loyalty and scores fetched concurrently
 1351:     );
 1352: 
 1353: Requirements and caveats:
 1354: 
 1355: =over 4
 1356: 
 1357: =item *
 1358: 
 1359: The C<threads> module must be available.  Most distributions ship it, but it
 1360: requires a Perl binary compiled with C<-Dusethreads>.  When threads are
 1361: unavailable, a C<carp> warning is emitted and fetching falls back to
 1362: sequential; the result is identical, only slower.
 1363: 
 1364: =item *
 1365: 
 1366: Parallel fetching is only active when the join has three or more total
 1367: databases (C<n E<gt> 2>).  With two databases (one secondary), the thread
 1368: creation overhead exceeds the benefit of concurrency; sequential is used
 1369: regardless of C<parallel>.
 1370: 
 1371: =item *
 1372: 
 1373: Component databases must be safe to call from Perl threads.  In-memory
 1374: databases (CSV, JSON, TSV after slurp) are safe.  DBI-backed databases
 1375: whose handles were created in the same thread may not be safe - consult your
 1376: DBD driver's thread documentation.  The array backend is recommended for
 1377: DBI-backed sources; the SQLite backend performs its join in a single SQL
 1378: statement and does not use parallel fetching.
 1379: 
 1380: =item *
 1381: 
 1382: C<parallel => 1> applies to the array backend only.  The SQLite backend
 1383: performs a single SQL JOIN after spilling source data, so per-DA parallelism
 1384: is irrelevant.
 1385: 
 1386: =back
 1387: 
 1388: B<Zero-copy ATTACH (C<dbi_source()> interface)>
 1389: 
 1390: Normally, when the SQLite path is active, rows from each component database
 1391: are fetched one by one and inserted into the temporary SQLite file.  This is
 1392: efficient but does involve INSERT overhead.
 1393: 
 1394: If a component database is itself SQLite-backed and implements a
 1395: C<dbi_source()> method, C<Database::Join> can skip the row-by-row copy
 1396: entirely and instead use C<ATTACH DATABASE> to link the source file directly
 1397: to the temporary join connection.  This is the zero-copy path and is
 1398: significantly faster for large SQLite sources.
 1399: 
 1400: The C<dbi_source()> method must return a hashref with two keys:
 1401: 
 1402: =over 4
 1403: 
 1404: =item C<dbh>
 1405: 
 1406: A connected C<DBD::SQLite> database handle (C<DBI> connection object).
 1407: 
 1408: =item C<table>
 1409: 
 1410: The name of the table in that database that holds the source rows.
 1411: 
 1412: =back
 1413: 
 1414: Example implementation:
 1415: 
 1416:     package My::SQLiteDatabase;
 1417:     use parent 'Database::Abstraction';
 1418: 
 1419:     sub dbi_source {
 1420:         my ($self) = @_;
 1421:         return {
 1422:             dbh   => $self->{_dbh},      # connected DBD::SQLite handle
 1423:             table => $self->{_table},    # table name in that database
 1424:         };
 1425:     }
 1426: 
 1427:     1;
 1428: 
 1429: The zero-copy ATTACH path is always used when a component database implements
 1430: C<dbi_source()>.  Query-time criteria are applied as parameterised SQL
 1431: C<WHERE> clauses against the ATTACHed source table, so no row-level copy is
 1432: needed even when the current call includes column filters.  (Prior to 0.005.0
 1433: any query-time criteria forced a spill; that restriction was removed in
 1434: 0.005.0.)
 1435: 
 1436: B<Result identity>
 1437: 
 1438: Both the array path and the SQLite path produce identical results for any
 1439: given query.  You can switch between them freely without changing callers.
 1440: The C<collision_prefix> column renaming, C<join_map> key translation, and all
 1441: three join types (left, inner, outer) work identically on both paths.
 1442: 
 1443: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 1444: 
 1445: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1446: 
 1447:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1448:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier  => 'gold');
 1449:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1450:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('C001');  # positional: entry => 'C001'
 1451:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(sort_by => 'name');
 1452:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => ['score', 'DESC']);
 1453:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10);
 1454:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10, offset => 20);
 1455:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => 'name', limit => 5);
 1456: 
 1457: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1458: 
 1459: Returns an arrayref of hashrefs representing the merged view of all component
 1460: databases, optionally filtered by the given criteria.
 1461: 
 1462: Criteria for columns that live in different databases are routed
 1463: automatically: each database is queried with only the criteria that apply to
 1464: its own columns.  The results are combined in memory using C<join_column>.
 1465: 
 1466: Accepts the same criteria syntax as C<Database::Abstraction::selectall_arrayref>.
 1467: A single plain scalar argument is interpreted as the C<join_column> value
 1468: (equivalent to C<< entry => 'C001' >> when C<join_column> is C<'entry'>).
 1469: 
 1470: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1471: 
 1472: =head4 Input
 1473: 
 1474:     Calling conventions (in order of precedence):
 1475:       1. No arguments             -- returns all rows
 1476:       2. One plain scalar         -- shorthand for join_column => $scalar
 1477:       3. Key-value pairs or
 1478:          a criteria hashref       -- routed per-database
 1479: 
 1480:     Values may be:
 1481:       Plain scalar                -- exact match
 1482:       Hashref of operators        -- e.g. { '>' => 80 }
 1483: 
 1484:     Optional parameters (mixed in with any of the above):
 1485:       sort_by => 'colname'            -- sort ascending by that column
 1486:       sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC']  -- sort descending
 1487:       sort_by => ['colname', 'ASC']   -- sort ascending (explicit)
 1488:       limit    => N                    -- return at most N rows (positive integer)
 1489:       offset   => M                   -- skip the first M rows (non-negative integer)
 1490: 
 1491:     The column named in sort_by must be present in the merged view (i.e. it
 1492:     must appear in columns()).  An unknown column or an invalid direction emits
 1493:     a carp warning and falls back to the default join_column ascending sort.
 1494: 
 1495:     limit and offset are applied after ordering.  offset without limit skips
 1496:     rows but returns all remaining rows.  limit without offset starts from
 1497:     the first qualifying row.  An invalid limit or offset emits a carp warning
 1498:     and the parameter is ignored (treated as absent).
 1499: 
 1500:     DOMAIN -- sort_by:
 1501:       EP absent:          result sorted by join_column ASC (default).
 1502:       EP string:          any column name in columns(); sorts ASC by that column.
 1503:       EP ['col','ASC']:   explicit ascending; equivalent to the string form.
 1504:       EP ['col','DESC']:  descending sort by the named column.
 1505:       EP ['col']:         single-element arrayref; direction defaults to ASC.
 1506:       EP []:              empty arrayref; column is undef -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1507:       EP invalid column:  column not in columns() -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1508:       EP invalid dir:     direction not 'ASC' or 'DESC' -> carp + ASC used.
 1509:       Sort is lexicographic (cmp); use backend=>'sqlite' for numeric ORDER BY.
 1510: 
 1511:     DOMAIN -- limit:
 1512:       EP absent:          no truncation; all qualifying rows are returned.
 1513:       EP 0:               not a positive integer -> carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1514:       BVA min valid = 1:  exactly 1 row returned.
 1515:       BVA at count:       limit == total rows -> all rows returned (no truncation).
 1516:       BVA above count:    limit > total rows -> all rows returned.
 1517:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1518:                           => carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1519:       Valid domain:       integers in [1, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a with value >= 1.
 1520: 
 1521:     DOMAIN -- offset:
 1522:       EP absent:          no rows skipped; result starts from row 0.
 1523:       BVA min valid = 0:  no rows skipped (zero is a valid non-negative integer).
 1524:       BVA offset=1:       first row skipped; result starts from row 1.
 1525:       BVA offset=N-1:     N-1 rows skipped; only the last row returned.
 1526:       BVA offset=N:       all N rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1527:       BVA offset>N:       all rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1528:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1529:                           => carp + ignored (no rows skipped).
 1530:       Valid domain:       integers in [0, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a.
 1531: 
 1532: =head4 Output
 1533: 
 1534:     Arrayref of hashrefs; one hashref per qualifying merged row.
 1535:     Sorted ascending by join_column by default; caller-controlled via sort_by.
 1536:     At most C<limit> rows when limit is given; the first C<offset> rows are
 1537:     skipped when offset is given.
 1538:     Returns a reference to an empty array when no rows match.
 1539: 
 1540: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1541: 
 1542:     # All rows from both databases
 1543:     my $all = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1544: 
 1545:     # Only rows where the 'tier' column (from the loyalty database)
 1546:     # equals 'gold' -- the criterion is routed to the right database
 1547:     my $vip = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1548: 
 1549:     # Operator hashref: score > 80
 1550:     my $high = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1551: 
 1552:     # Access each merged row
 1553:     for my $row (@{$vip}) {
 1554:         printf "%-10s tier=%-8s score=%d\n",
 1555:             $row->{entry}, $row->{tier}, $row->{score} // 0;
 1556:     }
 1557: 
 1558: =head3 MESSAGES
 1559: 
 1560:     warn_unknown_column (carp)
 1561:         -- A criterion key names a column not present in any component database;
 1562:            the criterion is silently dropped and all rows are returned.
 1563:     error_join_criterion (croak)
 1564:         -- A criterion hashref contains a join => key (DA SQL-JOIN syntax).
 1565:            Use a separate DA object for the joined table instead.
 1566:     sort_by column unknown (carp)
 1567:         -- The column given in sort_by is not in the merged view; the result
 1568:            is returned in the default join_column ascending order instead.
 1569:     sort_by direction invalid (carp)
 1570:         -- The direction given in sort_by is not 'ASC' or 'DESC'; ASC is used.
 1571:     limit invalid (carp)
 1572:         -- The value given for limit is not a positive integer; it is ignored.
 1573:     offset invalid (carp)
 1574:         -- The value given for offset is not a non-negative integer; it is ignored.
 1575:     operator-unsupported (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1576:         -- An operator hashref key is not in the supported set (>, <, >=, <=,
 1577:            !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE, IS NULL, IS NOT NULL, IN, NOT IN); the
 1578:            individual operator term is dropped from the WHERE clause (other
 1579:            operators in the same hashref still apply).
 1580:     IN/NOT IN wrong value type (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1581:         -- An IN or NOT IN criterion was given a non-arrayref value; the operator
 1582:            term is skipped.
 1583:     error_sqlite_connect (croak, SQLite/auto path only)
 1584:         -- The temporary SQLite join file could not be created; check tmpdir
 1585:            permissions and available disk space.
 1586: 
 1587: =cut
 1588:

 1602: =head2 selectall_array
 1603:

 1605:

 1612:

 1616:

 1741: =head2 count
 1742:

 1744:

 1749: 
 1750: Returns the number of merged rows that satisfy the given criteria.
 1751: 
 1752: On the array backend, the full in-memory join is performed and the resulting
 1753: rows are counted in Perl.  On the SQLite backend, a C<SELECT COUNT(*)> SQL
 1754: query is executed against the cached join tables, avoiding a full row fetch.
 1755: 
 1756: =head3 API SPECIFICATION

 1765: 
 1766: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1767: 
 1768:     my $total   = $join->count();

 1770:     my $high    = $join->count(score => { '>' => 90 });
 1771: 
 1772:     printf "%d total, %d gold-tier, %d high-scorers\n",
 1773:         $total, $gold, $high;
 1774: 
 1775: =head3 MESSAGES
 1776: 
 1777: Same messages as C<selectall_arrayref>.
 1778: 
 1779: =cut

 1792: 
 1793: =head2 aggregate
 1794: 
 1795: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1796: 
 1797:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 1798:         by       => ['tier'],
 1799:         measures => [
 1800:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum', as => 'total' },
 1801:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'   },
 1802:         ],
 1803:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
 1804:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 1805:         limit    => 10,
 1806:     );
 1807:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
 1808: 
 1809: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1810: 
 1811: Groups the merged view by one or more published column names and computes
 1812: aggregate measures over each group.
 1813: 
 1814: On the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>) and the C<auto> backend,
 1815: the aggregation is pushed into the existing SQLite cache as a C<GROUP BY>
 1816: query - rows never pass through Perl.  The C<auto> backend always takes this
 1817: path for aggregation, skipping the row-count threshold probe (aggregation
 1818: makes the row count irrelevant).
 1819: 
 1820: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), merged rows are fetched in
 1821: full and folded in Perl.  This materialises the complete matching result set.
 1822: 
 1823: The two paths return the same rows for the same input, except for two
 1824: documented differences: C<min>/C<max> on a column declared C<TEXT> but
 1825: storing numbers (SQL compares lexically; Perl detects numerics), and
 1826: C<sum>/C<avg> with non-numeric values (SQL coerces to 0.0; Perl skips and
 1827: warns via C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric>).
 1828: 
 1829: C<Database::Join> has no C<no_fixate> and no CHI cache (unlike
 1830: C<Database::Abstraction>); aggregation results are not cached between calls.
 1831: 
 1832: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1833: 
 1834: =head4 Input
 1835: 
 1836: Reserved keys extracted before the rest is treated as WHERE criteria:
 1837: 
 1838:     by       => arrayref of published column names (default: [] = grand total)
 1839:     measures => arrayref of measure hashrefs (required, non-empty)
 1840:     having   => hashref of post-aggregation criteria
 1841:     sort_by  => 'col' or ['col', 'DESC']
 1842:     limit    => positive integer
 1843:     offset   => non-negative integer
 1844: 
 1845: Every other key is pre-aggregation criteria routed exactly as
 1846: C<selectall_arrayref> routes criteria.
 1847: 
 1848: Measure hashref keys:
 1849: 
 1850:     fn  => function name (sum|avg|min|max|count|count_distinct), case-insensitive
 1851:     col => published column name (required for all functions except bare count)
 1852:     as  => result key (required; must match C</^\w+\z/>; unique; not a by name)
 1853: 
 1854: C<having> uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>:
 1855: C<%SAFE_SQL_OPS> (E<gt>, E<lt>, E<gt>=, E<lt>=, !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE),
 1856: C<%SAFE_LIST_OPS> (IN, NOT IN), C<%SAFE_NOARG_OPS> (IS NULL, IS NOT NULL),
 1857: a bare scalar (= ?), and C<-or>/C<-and> groupings.  Write C<-between> as
 1858: two operators and C<LIKE> explicitly.
 1859: 
 1860: =head4 Output
 1861: 
 1862: Always an arrayref of hashrefs (never C<undef>).  C<[]> when no group
 1863: qualifies and C<by> is non-empty.  When C<by> is omitted or C<[]> and no
 1864: rows match, returns one all-normalised row (grand total of zero).
 1865: 
 1866: Key order per row: C<by> columns in declaration order, then measure aliases
 1867: in declaration order.
 1868: 
 1869: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1870: 
 1871:     # Total score and row count per tier, highest total first
 1872:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 1873:         by       => ['tier'],
 1874:         measures => [
 1875:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
 1876:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
 1877:         ],
 1878:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 1879:     );
 1880:     for my $r (@{$rows}) {
 1881:         printf "%s: total=%s n=%s\n", $r->{tier}, $r->{total}, $r->{n};
 1882:     }
 1883: 
 1884: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 1885: 
 1886:     1. get_params(undef, @args) -- hashref or kv-pairs only (no positional key)
 1887:     2. croak error_join_criterion if 'join =>' present
 1888:     3. _validate_aggregate_spec extracts by/measures/having/sort_by/limit/offset
 1889:     4. array backend:
 1890:          rows   = _joined_query_array(remaining criteria, no sort/limit/offset)
 1891:          groups = _fold_aggregate(rows, spec)
 1892:          groups = [grep { _match_criteria($_, having) } @groups] if having
 1893:          _normalise_aggregate(groups, spec.measures)
 1894:          _sort_and_page(groups, spec)
 1895:        sqlite/auto backend:
 1896:          _sqlite_aggregate(remaining criteria, spec)
 1897:     5. return groups
 1898: 
 1899: =head3 MESSAGES
 1900: 
 1901: C<error_aggregate_measures_required>, C<error_aggregate_measure_ref>,
 1902: C<error_aggregate_fn_unknown>, C<error_aggregate_col_required>,
 1903: C<error_aggregate_col_unknown>, C<error_aggregate_as_required>,
 1904: C<error_aggregate_as_invalid>, C<error_aggregate_as_duplicate>,
 1905: C<error_aggregate_as_collision>, C<error_aggregate_by_invalid>,
 1906: C<error_aggregate_having_key>, C<error_aggregate_having_operator>,
 1907: C<warn_aggregate_sort_key>, C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric>.
 1908: 
 1909: =cut

 1942: 
 1943: =head2 each_row
 1944: 
 1945: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1946: 
 1947:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { my ($row) = @_; ... });
 1948:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, tier => 'gold');
 1949:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, sort_by => 'name', limit => 100);
 1950: 
 1951: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1952: 
 1953: Calls C<\&callback> once for every row in the merged view that matches the
 1954: given criteria, then returns the total count of rows visited.
 1955: 
 1956: Accepts the same criteria, C<sort_by>, C<limit>, and C<offset> parameters as
 1957: C<selectall_arrayref>.
 1958: 
 1959: B<Note on memory usage>: C<Database::Join> always materialises the complete
 1960: merged result set before invoking the callback, because merging rows from
 1961: multiple independent sources requires that every source be queried and
 1962: cross-referenced first.  True constant-memory streaming (as C<Database::Abstraction>
 1963: provides on single-source SQL queries) is not possible at the join layer.
 1964: For genuinely large result sets use the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt>
 1965: 'sqlite'>), which keeps peak RAM to approximately one times the source data
 1966: size rather than three.
 1967: 
 1968: Any exception thrown inside the callback propagates to the caller after the
 1969: current row; subsequent rows are not visited.
 1970: 
 1971: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1972: 
 1973: =head4 Input
 1974: 
 1975:     \&callback   Positional coderef (required).
 1976:                  Called as $callback->($row_hashref) for each merged row.
 1977: 
 1978:     Additional arguments follow the same calling conventions as
 1979:     selectall_arrayref: no args, a single scalar join-column value,
 1980:     or key-value criteria pairs (including sort_by, limit, offset).
 1981: 
 1982: =head4 Output
 1983: 
 1984:     Non-negative integer: the count of rows for which the callback was invoked.
 1985: 
 1986: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1987: 
 1988:     # Print every gold-tier customer's name, sorted by name
 1989:     my $count = $join->each_row(
 1990:         sub { my ($row) = @_; print "$row->{name}\n" },
 1991:         tier    => 'gold',
 1992:         sort_by => 'name',
 1993:     );
 1994:     print "$count gold-tier customers\n";
 1995: 
 1996:     # Accumulate without holding the full result
 1997:     my $total_score = 0;
 1998:     $join->each_row(sub { $total_score += $_[0]->{score} // 0 });
 1999: 
 2000: =head3 MESSAGES
 2001: 
 2002:     error_invalid_callback (croak)
