 2023: =head2 dbi_source
 2024: 
 2025: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2026: 
 2027:     # Use a Database::Join object as a zero-copy SQLite source inside a
 2028:     # parent Database::Join, giving the parent ATTACHed-speed access to
 2029:     # the child's joined data without iterating through Perl.
 2030:     my $child  = Database::Join->new(databases => [$da, $db], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2031:     my $parent = Database::Join->new(databases => [$child, $dc], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2032: 
 2033: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2034: 
 2035: Returns a hashref C<< { dbh => $sqlite_dbh, table => '_dj_result' } >> that
 2036: allows a parent C<Database::Join> (or any other caller that understands the
 2037: C<dbi_source()> interface) to ATTACH the child's temporary SQLite database
 2038: file and query the materialised join result directly via SQL, without routing
 2039: rows through Perl.
 2040: 
 2041: The first call builds the SQLite cache (if not already current) and
 2042: materialises the full join result - with C<filters> applied but no query-time
 2043: criteria - into a real table named C<_dj_result> inside the cache file.
 2044: Subsequent calls within the same cache cycle reuse the existing table.
 2045: 
 2046: Returns C<undef> when the backend is C<'array'> (no SQLite file exists).
 2047: 
 2048: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2049: 
 2050: =head4 Input
 2051: 
 2052:     None.
 2053: 
 2054: =head4 Output
 2055: 
 2056:     On the SQLite/auto backend:
 2057:       Hashref with keys:
 2058:         dbh   => DBI handle to the child's temporary SQLite database file.
 2059:         table => '_dj_result'  (the materialized join table inside that file).
 2060:     On the array backend:
 2061:       undef
 2062: 
 2063: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2064: 
 2065:     # The parent automatically ATTACHes the child's SQLite file and queries
 2066:     # _dj_result for zero-copy composable nested joins.
 2067:     my $inner = Database::Join->new(
 2068:         databases   => [$customers, $loyalty],
 2069:         join_column => 'entry',
 2070:         backend     => 'sqlite',
 2071:     );
 2072:     my $outer = Database::Join->new(
 2073:         databases   => [$inner, $scores],
 2074:         join_column => 'entry',
 2075:         backend     => 'sqlite',
 2076:     );
 2077:     my $rows = $outer->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 2078: 
 2079: =head3 MESSAGES
 2080: 
 2081:     error_sqlite_connect (croak)
 2082:         -- The temporary SQLite join file could not be created.
 2083: 
 2084: =cut
 2085:

 2274:

 2282: 
 2283: =head3 EXAMPLE

 2290:

 2295: only defined return values contribute to the maximum.  If no component database
 2296: implements C<updated()>, C<undef> is returned (same as C<List::Util::max> on an

 2379: =head2 add_database
 2380: 
 2381: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2382: 
 2383:     # Positional: database object as first argument
 2384:     $join->add_database($db);
 2385: 
 2386:     # Named: equivalent to the above
 2387:     $join->add_database(database => $db);
 2388: 
 2389:     # With options (mixed positional + named)
 2390:     $join->add_database($db, remove_columns => ['internal_id']);
 2391:     $join->add_database($db, join_column    => 'local_key_name');
 2392:     $join->add_database($db, filter         => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2393: 
 2394:     # Chainable
 2395:     $join->add_database($db1)->add_database($db2, remove_columns => ['notes']);
 2396: 
 2397: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2398: 
 2399: Adds one more C<Database::Abstraction> subclass object to the logical view
 2400: and immediately updates the column-ownership index.
 2401: 
 2402: After the call, all query methods return rows that include columns from the
 2403: newly added database, and criteria on those new columns are routed to it
 2404: automatically.
 2405: 
 2406: When a column name in the new database already exists in an earlier database,
 2407: the new database becomes the authoritative source for that column
 2408: (last-database-wins, the same rule that applies at construction time).
 2409: 
 2410: The join-column must be present in the new database (or declared via
 2411: C<join_column>).  The logger is propagated to the new database if one is set.
 2412: 
 2413: C<add_database> is the runtime equivalent of listing the database in the
 2414: C<databases> array to C<new>.  The optional C<join_column> parameter is
 2415: equivalent to a C<join_map> entry; the optional C<filter> parameter is
 2416: equivalent to a C<filters> entry.
 2417: 
 2418: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2419: 
 2420: =head4 Input
 2421: 
 2422:     database       => { type => 'object',   required => 1 }
 2423:                       # A Database::Abstraction subclass instance.
 2424:                       #
 2425:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   blessed object that passes
 2426:                       #                       isa('Database::Abstraction').
 2427:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-reference, unblessed ref, wrong class,
 2428:                       #                       or non-reference non-key scalar (the guard at
 2429:                       #                       the top of add_database rejects it with
 2430:                       #                       error_invalid_db before validate_strict runs).
 2431: 
 2432:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
 2433:                       # The name of the join key in THIS new database,
 2434:                       # when it differs from the canonical join_column.
 2435:                       #
 2436:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string that exists as a column in the
 2437:                       #                       new database.
 2438:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: string absent from the new database's columns()
 2439:                       #                       => croak error_join_col_missing.
 2440: 
 2441:     filter         => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
 2442:                       # Permanent criteria for this database only.
 2443:                       # Same format as selectall_arrayref.
 2444:                       #
 2445:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of criteria (may be {} for no-op).
 2446:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  no permanent filter applied; all rows visible.
 2447:                       # DOMAIN -- Key-set:    a non-empty filter makes this DB an inner-join
 2448:                       #                       partner regardless of the outer join_type.
 2449: 
 2450:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
 2451:                       # Column names from this database to hide.
 2452:                       #
 2453:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of strings; non-existent columns silently
 2454:                       #                       ignored; empty [] is a safe no-op.
 2455:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2456: 
 2457: =head4 Output
 2458: 
 2459:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 2460: 
 2461: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2462: 
 2463:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 2464:         databases   => [ $customers ],
 2465:         join_column => 'entry',
 2466:     );
 2467: 
 2468:     # Add loyalty data; hide internal columns from it
 2469:     $join->add_database($loyalty, remove_columns => ['audit_ts']);
 2470: 
 2471:     # Add score data; only include rows with score > 60
 2472:     $join->add_database($scores, filter => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2473: 
 2474:     # Add a database whose join key has a different local name
 2475:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'state_code');
 2476: 
 2477:     # All three options combined, and chained
 2478:     $join->add_database($db4,
 2479:         join_column    => 'ref_id',
 2480:         filter         => { active => 1 },
 2481:         remove_columns => ['legacy_col'],
 2482:     );
 2483: 
 2484: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2485: 
 2486:     determine the new database's index (length of current _dbs array)
 2487:     extract the database object from positional or named argument
 2488:     croak if it is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
 2489:     register join_column alias in _join_map if different from canonical
 2490:     register filter in _filters if provided
 2491:     fetch column list from the new database
 2492:     croak if the join key is missing from the new database
 2493:     append the new database to _dbs and _db_cols
 2494:     update _col_db: for each new column, point it at the new index
 2495:         (last-database-wins; skip removed columns and the local join alias)
 2496:     invalidate _col_cache and _schema_cache
 2497:     propagate logger if set
 2498:     apply remove_columns if provided
 2499:     return $self
 2500: 
 2501: =head3 MESSAGES
 2502: 
 2503:     error_invalid_db          -- argument is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
 2504:     error_join_col_missing    -- join_column not found in the new database
 2505:     warn_schema_type_mismatch -- (carp) the new database has a shared column whose type
 2506:                                  differs from the type already in the view; use
 2507:                                  collision_prefix to preserve both values
 2508: 
 2509: =cut

 2616: 
 2617: =head2 remove_column
 2618: 
 2619: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2620: 
 2621:     $join->remove_column('email');
 2622: 
 2623:     # Chainable
 2624:     $join->remove_column('internal_id')->remove_column('audit_ts');
 2625: 
 2626: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2627: 
 2628: Permanently hides a column from the merged view.  After this call:
 2629: 
 2630: =over 4
 2631: 
 2632: =item *
 2633: 
 2634: The column does not appear in C<columns()> or C<schema()>.
 2635: 
 2636: =item *
 2637: 
 2638: Returned row hashrefs do not contain the column key.
 2639: 
 2640: =item *
 2641: 
 2642: Any query criterion that references the removed column is silently dropped
 2643: (with a C<carp> warning).
 2644: 
 2645: =back
 2646: 
 2647: The C<join_column> cannot be removed; attempting to do so will C<croak>.
 2648: Removing a column that does not exist in any database is silently ignored
 2649: (the call is idempotent and safe).  The C<columns()> and C<schema()>
 2650: memoisation caches are cleared automatically.
 2651: 
 2652: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2653: 
 2654: =head4 Input
 2655: 
 2656:     $col    Positional string: the column name to remove.
 2657: 
 2658:             DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string; non-existent columns are silently
 2659:                                   ignored (idempotent call, returns $self).
 2660:             DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column value => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2661:             DOMAIN -- BVA:        undef and '' are explicit no-ops (returns $self).
 2662:                                   These are below the minimum meaningful string
 2663:                                   length and are handled without any warning.
 2664: 
 2665: =head4 Output
 2666: 
 2667:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 2668: 
 2669: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2670: 
 2671:     # Hide private fields immediately after construction
 2672:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 2673:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
 2674:         join_column => 'entry',
 2675:     )->remove_column('email')
 2676:      ->remove_column('internal_notes');
 2677: 
 2678:     # Verify they are gone
 2679:     my $cols = $join->columns();
 2680:     # 'email' and 'internal_notes' are absent
 2681: 
 2682: =head3 MESSAGES
 2683: 
 2684:     error_remove_join_col -- attempt to remove the join_column itself
 2685: 
 2686: =cut
 2687: 
 2688: sub remove_column {
 2689: 	my ($self, $col) = @_;
 2690: 
 2691: 	# Premise: undef and '' are provably no-ops (nothing to remove).
 2692: 	# Conclusion: guard at the top eliminates two separate defined() checks below.
 2693: 	return $self unless defined $col && length $col;

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2694: 
 2695: 	# Premise: $col is defined (proven above) and join_col is always a non-empty string.
 2696: 	# Conclusion: direct string comparison is safe without a redundant defined() check.
 2697: 	croak $self->_err('error_remove_join_col', $col)
 2698: 		if $col eq $self->{_join_col};
 2699: 
 2700: 	$self->{_removed_cols}{$col} = 1;
 2701: 	delete $self->{_col_db}{$col};
 2702: 	$self->{_col_cache}    = undef;
 2703: 	$self->{_schema_cache} = undef;
 2704: 	$self->{_removed_list} = undef;	# invalidate the cached removed-column list
 2705: 
 2706: 	return $self;

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 2707: }

 2709: =head2 query
 2710: 
 2711: Not supported.  C<Database::Join> does not implement the
 2712: C<Database::Abstraction::Query> chained builder because the builder's
 2713: C<.all()> / C<.first()> methods would be targeting a single component DA
 2714: rather than the merged view.  Calling this method will always C<croak>.
 2715: 
 2716: Use C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>,
 2717: C<count>, or C<each_row> against the C<Database::Join> object instead.
 2718: 
 2719: =cut
 2720:

 2722: 	my $self = $_[0];
 2723: 	croak $self->_err('error_query_unsupported');
 2724: }
 2725: 
 2726: =head2 execute
 2727: 
 2728: Not supported.  Raw SQL cannot span heterogeneous backends that may use
 2729: different database engines.  Calling this method will always C<croak>.
 2730: 
 2731: Use C<selectall_arrayref> or C<fetchrow_hashref> to query the joined view.
 2732: 
 2733: =cut
 2734:

 2739: 
 2740: =head2 AUTOLOAD - column shortcut
 2741: 
 2742: Calling an unknown method whose name matches a visible column name performs
 2743: a column lookup across the merged view.
 2744: 
 2745: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2746: 
 2747:     # Scalar context: value from the first matching row
 2748:     my $name  = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
 2749: 
 2750:     # List context: values from every matching row
 2751:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
 2752: 
 2753:     # With a positional join-key argument (when join_column is 'entry')
 2754:     my $score = $join->score('C001');
 2755: 
 2756: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2757: 
 2758: AUTOLOAD routes the call to the appropriate component database by looking up
 2759: the column name in the internal column-ownership index.
 2760: 
 2761: When either C<join_map> or C<filters> is active, AUTOLOAD performs a full
 2762: join query instead of delegating directly to the owning database.  This is
 2763: necessary because:
 2764: 
 2765: =over 4
 2766: 
 2767: =item *
 2768: 
 2769: With C<join_map>, the owning database's primary key may differ from the
 2770: canonical join key used in the call arguments.
 2771: 
 2772: =item *
 2773: 
 2774: With C<filters>, bypassing the join would return rows that the filter is
 2775: meant to exclude.
 2776: 
 2777: =back
 2778: 
 2779: In list context, every matching merged row contributes one value to the
 2780: returned list.  In scalar context, only the first row's value is returned.
 2781: 
 2782: Calling a method whose name begins with C<_> (a private method) via AUTOLOAD
 2783: will C<croak> with a clear error message rather than being silently ignored.
 2784: 
 2785: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2786: 
 2787:     # Lookup a single customer's name (scalar context)
 2788:     my $name = $join->name('C001');   # 'C001' maps to entry => 'C001'
 2789:     print "Name: $name\n";
 2790: 
 2791:     # Get every tier value in the view (list context)
 2792:     my @all_tiers = $join->tier();
 2793:     my %freq;
 2794:     $freq{$_}++ for @all_tiers;
 2795: 
 2796:     # join_map active: AUTOLOAD runs a full join so the criteria are
 2797:     # translated correctly between the canonical and local key names.
 2798:     my @leesburg_states = sort $join->state('Leesburg');
 2799:     # ['Florida', 'Virginia'] if Leesburg appears in two states
 2800: 
 2801: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2802: 
 2803:     extract column name from $AUTOLOAD
 2804:     return if DESTROY
 2805:     croak if column name starts with '_' (private method guard)
 2806:     croak if column name is not in _col_db (unknown column)
 2807:     if join_map or filters are active:
 2808:         parse calling arguments using _parse_query_args
 2809:         call _joined_query to get all merged rows
 2810:         return map { $_->{col} } @rows  in list context
 2811:         return $rows[0]{col}            in scalar context
 2812:     else:
 2813:         delegate directly to the owning database
 2814: 
 2815: =head3 MESSAGES
 2816: 
 2817:     (croak) Database::Join: cannot call private method '_NAME' via AUTOLOAD
 2818:         -- Method name begins with '_'.  Private methods must be called directly,
 2819:            not via AUTOLOAD.  This is a programming error.
 2820: 
 2821:     (croak) Database::Join: unknown column 'NAME'
 2822:         -- Method name does not match any visible column in the merged view.
 2823:            Check spelling, or whether the column was removed with remove_column().
 2824: 
 2825: =cut
 2826: 
 2827: our $AUTOLOAD;
 2828:

 3042: # _partition_criteria( \%params ) -> \@per_db
 3043: # Purpose: Split a flat criteria hashref into one slice per component database.

 3126: # _joined_query_array( \%params ) -> \@merged_rows
 3127: #
 3128: # Purpose: Core in-memory join algorithm.  Partitions criteria, fetches per-database
 3129: #          results, resolves the key set, and merges rows.
 3130: #
 3131: # Key-set resolution (applied for each secondary database after the primary):
 3132: #
 3133: #   If the database had criteria in this query call (after base filter overlay),
 3134: #   it acts as an INNER-JOIN partner: only keys present in its filtered result
 3135: #   survive.  This gives WHERE-clause semantics even under a LEFT join.
 3136: #
 3137: #   If the database had NO effective criteria:
 3138: #     inner  -> intersect  (standard inner join)
 3139: #     left   -> no change  (primary defines the key set)
 3140: #     outer  -> union      (all keys from any database)
 3141: #
 3142: # Row merge: for each qualifying primary row, secondary rows are overlaid in
 3143: # index order.  For duplicate columns, later databases win.  Local join-key
 3144: # aliases are renamed to the canonical join_column before merging.
 3145: # Removed columns are deleted from every merged row.

 4898: # _copy_criteria( \%criteria ) -> \%copy
 4899: # Purpose: Return a two-level deep copy of a single criteria hashref so that
 4900: #          post-construction mutation of the caller's hash cannot change the
 4901: #          stored filter.  Operator sub-hashrefs (e.g. { '>' => 80 }) are
 4902: #          shallow-copied one additional level, matching the broadcast-copy
 4903: #          idiom used in _partition_criteria for join-column criteria.
 4904: # Entry:   $criteria is a hashref (may be undef).
 4905: # Exit:    Returns a new hashref; never returns the input reference itself.

 4911: 			$_ => ref($criteria->{$_}) eq 'HASH'
 4912: 				? { %{ $criteria->{$_} } }  # shallow-copy operator sub-hashref
 4913: 				: $criteria->{$_}
 4914: 		} keys %{$criteria}
 4915: 	};
 4916: }

 4951: 
 4952: =head1 ENCODING
 4953: 
 4954: C<Database::Join> is a routing and merge layer written in pure Perl.  It never
 4955: inspects, re-encodes, or transforms the I<content> of strings -- only their
 4956: identity (via C<eq> / hash-key equality) and, on the array backend, their
 4957: numeric interpretation (via C<looks_like_number>).
 4958: 
 4959: =over 4
 4960: 
 4961: =item Column names
 4962: 
 4963: Column names are exactly what each component C<Database::Abstraction> object
 4964: returns from its own C<columns()> method.  C<Database::Join> compares them with
 4965: C<eq> (byte-level equality) and builds its internal routing index from them.
 4966: ASCII column names (letters, digits, underscore) are safe everywhere.
 4967: Non-ASCII column names work as long as every component database returns them
 4968: in the same byte encoding and your Perl source file is loaded in the same
 4969: encoding.  Mixed encodings between component databases are not supported.
 4970: 
 4971: =item Join-key values (the join_column)
 4972: 
 4973: The join-column value is used as a hash key internally (C<$hash{$key}>).
 4974: Perl uses byte-level equality for hash keys, so any value that round-trips
 4975: correctly through your component databases works as a join key, including
 4976: non-ASCII strings, emoji, and other Unicode text, as long as all component
 4977: databases return the same bytes for the same conceptual key.
 4978: 
 4979:     # A Japanese city name as a join key works fine
 4980:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(entry => "\x{6771}\x{4EAC}");
 4981: 
 4982: =item Criteria values and row data
 4983: 
 4984: Criterion values and merged row values are passed verbatim between the caller
 4985: and the component databases.  C<Database::Join> does not examine them.  Full
 4986: UTF-8 text (including multi-byte characters, accented letters, CJK ideographs,
 4987: and emoji) round-trips safely provided the underlying component databases handle
 4988: UTF-8 correctly.
 4989: 
 4990:     # Emoji in a criterion value -- works if the DA supports it
 4991:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(mood => "\x{1F600}");
 4992: 
 4993: =item SQLite backend: UTF-8 round-trip guarantee
 4994: 
 4995: When the SQLite path is active (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> or C<'auto'>),
 4996: values are inserted into the temporary C<DBD::SQLite> database exclusively
 4997: through DBI prepared-statement placeholders -- never by string interpolation.
 4998: C<DBD::SQLite> operates in UTF-8 mode by default (C<sqlite_unicode =E<gt> 1>
 4999: in newer releases), so multi-byte UTF-8 text round-trips without corruption.
 5000: Binary blobs containing null bytes may not round-trip correctly; use the array
 5001: backend if you need binary-safe joins.
 5002: 
 5003: =item Sorting on the array backend uses Perl string comparison
 5004: 
 5005: The array backend sorts with Perl's C<cmp> (byte-level lexicographic order).
 5006: This is correct for pure-ASCII data and for consistently-encoded UTF-8 data
 5007: where byte order matches Unicode code-point order (e.g. sorting within a
 5008: single script).  For locale-aware sorting (e.g. sorting German umlauts in
 5009: German alphabetical order) switch to the SQLite backend, which delegates to
 5010: SQLite's C<ORDER BY> (locale support depends on how SQLite was compiled).
 5011: 
 5012: =item Measure aliases in aggregate()
 5013: 
 5014: The C<as> alias for each measure must match C</^\w+\z/a> -- ASCII word
 5015: characters only (letters, digits, underscore).  Full Unicode identifiers
 5016: are not supported as aliases.
 5017: 
 5018: =item i18n messages
 5019: 
 5020: All internal error and warning messages route through the optional C<i18n>
 5021: object via a C<translate($key, @args)> call.  The translation dictionary
 5022: controls the final encoding and character set of those user-visible strings.
 5023: 
 5024: =back
 5025: 
 5026: =head1 MESSAGES
 5027: 
 5028: The following messages can be produced by C<Database::Join>.  All messages
 5029: can be localised by supplying an C<i18n> object to C<new>.
 5030: 
 5031: =over 4
 5032: 
 5033: =item C<error_no_databases>
 5034: 
 5035: B<When:> The C<databases> arrayref passed to C<new> is empty.
 5036: 
 5037: B<Fix:> Pass at least one C<Database::Abstraction> subclass object.
 5038: 
 5039: =item C<error_invalid_db>
 5040: 
 5041: B<When:> An element of the C<databases> array (or the argument to
 5042: C<add_database>) is not an object, or is not a C<Database::Abstraction>
 5043: subclass.
 5044: 
 5045: B<Fix:> Instantiate the component database with its own C<new> method before
 5046: passing it to C<Database::Join>.
 5047: 
 5048: =item C<error_join_col_missing>
 5049: 
 5050: B<When:> The join key column (or its C<join_map> alias) does not exist in one
 5051: of the component databases.
 5052: 
 5053: B<Fix:> Either add the column to the database, change C<join_column> to a
 5054: column that is present everywhere, or use C<join_map> to declare the local
 5055: alias for databases that call it something different.
 5056: 
 5057: =item C<error_remove_join_col>
 5058: 
 5059: B<When:> C<remove_column> is called with the name of the join key column.
 5060: 
 5061: B<Fix:> The join key is required for the merge to work and cannot be hidden.
 5062: Remove a different column.
 5063: 
 5064: =item C<error_invalid_prefix>
 5065: 
 5066: B<When:> A value in the C<collision_prefix> hashref is a reference (e.g. a
 5067: hashref or arrayref) rather than a plain string.
 5068: 
 5069: B<Fix:> All C<collision_prefix> values must be plain strings.  A reference
 5070: would be stringified to C<HASH(0x...)> or C<ARRAY(0x...)>, leaking a heap
 5071: address into every column name returned by C<columns()>, C<schema()>, and all
 5072: query results.  Pass a plain string such as C<'db2'> or C<'secondary'>.
 5073: 
 5074: =item C<warn_unknown_column> (carp)
 5075: 
 5076: B<When:> A criterion is passed for a column that does not exist in any
 5077: component database (or has been removed with C<remove_column>).
 5078: 
 5079: B<Fix:> Check the column name spelling.  The criterion is ignored.
 5080: 
 5081: =item C<error_join_criterion>
 5082: 
 5083: B<When:> A criterion hashref contains a C<join =E<gt>> key (the
 5084: C<Database::Abstraction> SQL-JOIN syntax).
 5085: 
 5086: B<Fix:> C<join =E<gt>> targets a single table inside one DA; it cannot be
 5087: routed through a merged view.  Express multi-table relationships by adding
 5088: the joined table as a separate C<Database::Abstraction> object in the
 5089: C<databases =E<gt> []> constructor list instead.
 5090: 
 5091: =item C<error_query_unsupported>
 5092: 
 5093: B<When:> C<query()> is called on a C<Database::Join> object.
 5094: 
 5095: B<Fix:> Use C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>,
 5096: or C<count> instead.
 5097: 
 5098: =item C<error_execute_unsupported>
 5099: 
 5100: B<When:> C<execute()> is called on a C<Database::Join> object.
 5101: 
 5102: B<Fix:> Use the Perl-level query methods instead.  Raw SQL cannot span
 5103: heterogeneous database backends.
 5104: 
 5105: =item C<error_invalid_backend>
 5106: 
 5107: B<When:> The C<backend> parameter passed to C<new> is not one of C<'array'>,
 5108: C<'sqlite'>, or C<'auto'>.
 5109: 
 5110: B<Fix:> Use exactly one of those three strings.  The check is case-sensitive;
 5111: C<'SQLite'> or C<'Auto'> will not be accepted.
 5112: 
 5113: =item C<error_sqlite_connect>
 5114: 
 5115: B<When:> The SQLite join backend fails to open the temporary SQLite database
 5116: file.  Common causes: the C<tmpdir> directory is not writable, the filesystem
 5117: has no free space, or C<DBD::SQLite> is not installed.
 5118: 
 5119: B<Fix:> Check that the directory given by C<tmpdir> (or the system temp
 5120: directory if C<tmpdir> was not set) is writable and has sufficient free space.
 5121: Verify that C<DBD::SQLite> 1.70 or later is installed.
 5122: 
 5123: =item C<error_aggregate_measures_required>
 5124: 
 5125: B<When:> C<aggregate()> is called without a C<measures> key, or with
 5126: C<measures => []>.
 5127: 
 5128: B<Fix:> Pass a non-empty arrayref: C<< measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }] >>.
 5129: 
 5130: =item C<error_aggregate_measure_ref>
 5131: 
 5132: B<When:> One of the elements in the C<measures> arrayref is not a hashref (it
 5133: is a scalar, arrayref, or other non-hash reference).
 5134: 
 5135: B<Fix:> Every element of C<measures> must be a plain hashref with at least
 5136: C<fn> and C<as> keys.
 5137: 
 5138: =item C<error_aggregate_fn_unknown>
 5139: 
 5140: B<When:> The C<fn> value in a measure hashref is not one of: C<sum>, C<avg>,
 5141: C<min>, C<max>, C<count>, C<count_distinct> (case-insensitive).
 5142: 
 5143: B<Fix:> Use a supported function name.  C<median>, C<stddev> and window
 5144: functions are not implemented.
 5145: 
 5146: =item C<error_aggregate_col_required>
 5147: 
 5148: B<When:> The C<col> field is missing from a measure whose C<fn> requires a
 5149: column (any function except plain C<count>).
 5150: 
 5151: B<Fix:> Add C<< col => 'column_name' >> to the measure hashref.  C<count>
 5152: without C<col> emits C<COUNT(*)>; all other functions require a column.
 5153: 
 5154: =item C<error_aggregate_col_unknown>
 5155: 
 5156: B<When:> The C<col> in a measure, or a name in C<by>, refers to a column that
 5157: is not in the merged view (either never existed or was hidden with
 5158: C<remove_column>).
 5159: 
 5160: B<Fix:> Check C<< $join->columns() >> for the correct published name.
 5161: C<collision_prefix>-renamed columns are accessed as C<"prefix.col">.
 5162: 
 5163: =item C<error_aggregate_as_required>
 5164: 
 5165: B<When:> A measure hashref has no C<as> key, or its value is C<undef> or the
 5166: empty string.
 5167: 
 5168: B<Fix:> Every measure must have a non-empty C<as> alias: C<< as => 'total' >>.
 5169: 
 5170: =item C<error_aggregate_as_invalid>
 5171: 
 5172: B<When:> A measure alias (C<as>) contains characters other than word
 5173: characters (C<\w>: letters, digits, underscore).
 5174: 
 5175: B<Fix:> Use a plain word for the alias: C<< as => 'total' >>, not
 5176: C<< as => 'total score' >> or C<< as => 'total-score' >>.
 5177: 
 5178: =item C<error_aggregate_as_duplicate>
 5179: 
 5180: B<When:> Two or more measures share the same C<as> alias.
 5181: 
 5182: B<Fix:> Choose a unique alias for each measure.
 5183: 
 5184: =item C<error_aggregate_as_collision>
 5185: 
 5186: B<When:> A measure's C<as> alias is the same as one of the C<by> column names.
 5187: 
 5188: B<Fix:> Choose an alias that does not match any grouping dimension.
 5189: 
 5190: =item C<error_aggregate_by_invalid>
 5191: 
 5192: B<When:> The C<by> parameter is present but is not an arrayref.
 5193: 
 5194: B<Fix:> Pass an arrayref (possibly empty for a grand total):
 5195: C<< by => ['region'] >> or C<< by => [] >>.
 5196: 
 5197: =item C<error_aggregate_having_key>
 5198: 
 5199: B<When:> A key in the C<having> hashref is not one of the C<by> column names,
 5200: a measure alias, C<-or>, or C<-and>.
 5201: 
 5202: B<Fix:> Only filter on names that appear in the aggregate output.
 5203: 
 5204: =item C<error_aggregate_having_operator>
 5205: 
 5206: B<When:> An operator hashref inside C<having> uses an operator that is not in
 5207: C<%SAFE_SQL_OPS>, C<%SAFE_LIST_OPS>, or C<%SAFE_NOARG_OPS>.
 5208: 
 5209: B<Fix:> Use a supported operator.  C<BETWEEN> is not supported; write it as
 5210: two constraints: C<< { '>=' => $lo, '<=' => $hi } >>.
 5211: 
 5212: =item C<warn_aggregate_sort_key> (carp)
 5213: 
 5214: B<When:> The column or alias named by C<sort_by> in C<aggregate()> is not one
 5215: of the C<by> columns or measure aliases.
 5216: 
 5217: B<Fix:> Use a column name from C<by> or a measure alias from C<measures>.
 5218: The sort falls back to the default group ordering (C<by> columns ascending).
 5219: 
 5220: =item C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric> (carp)
 5221: 
 5222: B<When:> The array backend encounters one or more non-numeric values in a
 5223: column that is the target of a C<sum> or C<avg> measure.  The SQLite backend
 5224: coerces non-numeric values to C<0.0> via C<CAST> and emits no warning.
 5225: 
 5226: B<Fix:> Clean the data before joining, or switch to the SQLite backend if
 5227: coercion to zero is acceptable.
 5228: 
 5229:     # Example: the warning names the column, the count, and the first bad value
 5230:     # "aggregate: column "score" has 2 non-numeric value(s), skipped, e.g. "n/a""
 5231: 
 5232: =back
 5233: 
 5234: =head1 REPOSITORY
 5235:

 5253: 
 5254: =head1 SECURITY CONSIDERATIONS
 5255: 
 5256: C<Database::Join> is a pure in-memory routing and merge layer.  It never
 5257: generates SQL strings, never opens files, and never calls C<system()>,
 5258: C<exec()>, or C<eval()>.  The security properties described below are
 5259: architectural guarantees, not run-time checks.
 5260: 
 5261: =head2 What Database::Join guarantees
 5262: 
 5263: =over 4
 5264: 
 5265: =item Criteria partition isolation
 5266: 
 5267: Every criterion you pass to a query method is routed to I<exactly one>
 5268: component database (the one that owns that column), or to I<all> databases
 5269: when the criterion is on the join key column.  A hostile value in a criterion
 5270: for column C<name> (owned by database A) will never reach database B.
 5271: 
 5272: =item Unknown columns are rejected before reaching any database
 5273: 
 5274: If a criterion column name is not present in any component database (or has
 5275: been hidden with C<remove_column>), C<Database::Join> logs a C<carp> warning
 5276: and silently drops the criterion.  No database receives the hostile key.
 5277: 
 5278: =item AUTOLOAD only accepts word-character column names
 5279: 
 5280: Perl's method dispatch extracts the column name via C<\w+>, which matches only
 5281: C<[A-Za-z0-9_]>.  Hostile method names with shell metacharacters, quotes, or
 5282: spaces cannot reach the AUTOLOAD dispatch path.  Private names (starting with
 5283: C<_>) are additionally blocked with an explicit C<croak>.
 5284: 
 5285: =item No value sanitisation (by design)
 5286: 
 5287: C<Database::Join> does I<not> sanitise, HTML-encode, or validate the
 5288: I<values> in criteria hashrefs.  Preventing SQL injection is the
 5289: responsibility of the underlying C<Database::Abstraction> objects (which use
 5290: parameterised queries).  Preventing XSS or header injection is the
 5291: responsibility of the CGI or web layer that renders the output.
 5292: 
 5293: =item Taint-mode compatible (array path)
 5294: 
 5295: The array merge path contains no C<system()>, C<exec()>, backtick,
 5296: C<open(PIPE)>, or C<eval STRING> calls.  It neither opens files nor constructs
 5297: shell commands.  The AUTOLOAD regex C</ :: (\w++) \z /x> uses a possessive
 5298: quantifier (C<\w++>) and a strict end-of-string anchor (C<\z>) and produces
 5299: an I<untainted> capture, so the column name used for dispatch is clean under
 5300: C<-T>.  Criteria values are passed verbatim to component C<Database::Abstraction>
 5301: objects; those objects are responsible for handling tainted values at the SQL
 5302: parameterisation layer.
 5303: 
 5304: =item SQLite backend: column names are quoted, values are parametrised
 5305: 
 5306: When the SQLite path is active, C<Database::Join> generates SQL internally.
 5307: All column and table names are double-quoted (SQL identifier quoting) before
 5308: being embedded in statement strings.  All row values are passed to SQLite
 5309: exclusively through DBI prepared statement placeholders -- never by string
 5310: interpolation.  A hostile value in a source row therefore cannot inject SQL
 5311: into the temporary database.
 5312: 
 5313: The temporary SQLite file is created by C<File::Temp> using a securely random,
 5314: unpredictable filename.  No C<system()> or shell command is used to create or
 5315: remove it.  The connection is made with C<DBI-E<gt>connect(..., { RaiseError =E<gt> 1,
 5316: PrintError =E<gt> 0 })> and is closed before the method returns.  Column names
 5317: in the generated SQL come from C<columns()>, which is produced by
 5318: C<Database::Abstraction> at construction time -- they are not derived from
 5319: caller-supplied criteria values.
 5320: 
 5321: =item Operator hashref broadcast copy
 5322: 
 5323: When the same join-key criterion (an operator hashref such as
 5324: C<< { '>' => 'A' } >>) is broadcast to multiple component databases, each
 5325: database receives its own I<shallow copy> of the hashref.  A component database
 5326: that mutates the hashref's contents at the top level cannot affect what
 5327: subsequent databases receive.
 5328: 
 5329: =item collision_prefix value type guard
 5330: 
 5331: C<_build_col_index> rejects any C<collision_prefix> value that is a reference
 5332: (hashref, arrayref, coderef, etc.) with an immediate C<croak>.  A reference
 5333: value would stringify to C<"HASH(0x...)">, leaking a heap address into every
 5334: column name, C<columns()> listing, and merged row returned to the caller.  The
 5335: guard fires before any column name is constructed.
 5336: 
 5337: =item Filter deep-copy isolation
 5338: 
 5339: The C<filters> constructor parameter and the C<filter> option of
 5340: C<add_database()> are I<deep-copied> at the point of use.  The caller's
 5341: original hashrefs are never stored; post-construction mutation of those
 5342: hashrefs cannot widen or bypass the configured row-security constraints.
 5343: 
 5344: =back
 5345: 
 5346: =head2 What the caller is responsible for
 5347: 
 5348: =over 4
 5349: 
 5350: =item Sanitise values before building criteria
 5351: 
 5352: DJ passes criterion values verbatim to component databases.  If your
 5353: application accepts user-supplied filter values (e.g. from a CGI query
 5354: string), those values I<must> be validated or sanitised by your application
 5355: before being passed to DJ.
 5356: 
 5357: =item Restrict which columns the caller can filter on
 5358: 
 5359: Any column in C<columns()> can be used as a filter criterion.  If a column
 5360: should not be filterable by end users (e.g. an internal status flag), hide it
 5361: with C<remove_column> so that queries on it are silently dropped.
 5362: 
 5363: =item Do not expose the joined view directly to user-supplied criteria
 5364: 
 5365: DJ is not a firewall.  It faithfully routes user input to component databases.
 5366: Wrap DJ calls in a thin service layer that whitelists the permitted criterion
 5367: columns and validates their values.
 5368: 
 5369: =back
 5370: 
 5371: =head3 API SPECIFICATION (security surface)
 5372: 
 5373:     Input accepted by all query methods and passed through DJ to component databases:
 5374: 
 5375:     Criterion values:
 5376:         type: scalar string | operator hashref { OP => scalar }
 5377:         validation: NONE (DJ trusts the caller; component DA is responsible)
 5378:         max size: unconstrained (OOM risk on very large values)
 5379: 
 5380:     Column name keys in criteria:
 5381:         type: string
 5382:         validation: must be present in _col_db (else carp + drop)
 5383:         character set: any Perl string (including control chars); DJ does
 5384:                        not impose a character-set restriction on criteria KEYS
 5385: 
 5386:     AUTOLOAD method-name-as-column:
 5387:         type: \w+ (enforced by Perl regex /::(\w+)$/)
 5388:         validation: must not start with '_'; must be in _col_db
 5389: 
 5390: =encoding UTF-8
 5391: 
 5392: =head1 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 5393: 
 5394: Z calculus schemas for the key invariants and operations.
 5395: Unicode is used throughout this section as required by Z notation.

 5494:     -- Only secondary databases (index > 0) carry a meaningful prefix:
 5495:     dom collision_prefix âŠ† 1 â€¥ (#dbs - 1)
 5496: 
 5497:     -- Published name for column col from database i:
 5498:     published(i, col) ==
 5499:         if i âˆˆ dom collision_prefix âˆ§ col âˆˆ dom col_db âˆ§ col_db(col) < i
 5500:         then (collision_prefix i) ^ "." ^ col
 5501:         else col

 5504:     col_db(published(i, col)) = i

 5506:     -- The original column name is never removed from an earlier database:
 5507:     âˆ€ i : 1 â€¥ #dbs-1; col : columns(dbs i) â€¢
 5508:         published(i, col) â‰  col  âŸ¹
 5509:             âˆƒ j : 0 â€¥ i-1 â€¢ col âˆˆ dom col_db âˆ§ col_db(col) = j

 5528: =head2 SECURITY INVARIANTS
 5529: 
 5530:     â”€â”€â”€ PartitionIsolation â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€

 5532:     âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢

 5537:         (âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ col âˆ‰ dom(per_db(i)))
 5538: 
 5539:     â”€â”€â”€ NoCodeExecution â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5540:     -- DJ contains no call to system(), exec(), open(PIPE), or eval().
 5541:     -- Hostile criterion values therefore cannot achieve code execution
 5542:     -- within the Database::Join layer.
 5543:     âˆ€ v : VALUE â€¢ _joined_query({col â†¦ v}) â‰  âŠ¥ due to code injection
 5544: 
 5545: =head2 filters
 5546: 
 5547:     â”€â”€â”€ Filters â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5548:     filters  : â„• â‡¸ CRITERIA
 5549:     dbs      : seq DATABASE_ABSTRACTION
 5550:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5551:     dom filters âŠ† 0 â€¥ (#dbs - 1)
 5552: 
 5553:     -- A filtered database i always contributes to key-set intersection.
 5554:     -- For each query with criteria C:
 5555:     effective_criteria(i, C) ==
 5556:         if i âˆˆ dom filters
 5557:         then merge_criteria(filters(i), partition(C, i))
 5558:         else partition(C, i)
 5559: 
 5560:     -- Criteria merging (AND semantics for operator hashrefs):
 5561:     merge_criteria(base, extra) ==
 5562:         { col : dom base âˆª dom extra â€¢
 5563:             if col âˆˆ dom base âˆ© dom extra
 5564:                âˆ§ base(col) âˆˆ HASHREF âˆ§ extra(col) âˆˆ HASHREF
 5565:             then col â†¦ base(col) âˆª extra(col)   -- operator union
 5566:             else col â†¦ (if col âˆˆ dom extra then extra(col) else base(col)) }
 5567: 
 5568: =head2 base_criteria
 5569: 
 5570:     â”€â”€â”€ BaseCriteria â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5571:     base_criteria : CRITERIA              -- column-name keyed
 5572:     col_db        : NAME â‡¸ â„•
 5573:     filters       : â„• â‡¸ CRITERIA         -- after Init
 5574:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5575:     -- base_criteria is applied once at construction by partitioning
 5576:     -- its columns into per-db slices and merging into filters:
 5577:     âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢
 5578:         bc_slice(i) = partition(base_criteria, i)
 5579:         filters(i)  = merge_criteria(bc_slice(i), filters_explicit(i))
 5580: 
 5581:     -- where filters_explicit is the filters parameter as supplied.
 5582:     -- merge_criteria semantics: explicit filters win on plain-scalar
 5583:     -- conflicts; operator hashrefs are combined (AND).
 5584: 
 5585:     -- Unknown columns are dropped (warn_unknown_column carp);
 5586:     -- the join_column is broadcast to all databases.
 5587: 
 5588:     -- Key-set semantics: any database that receives a non-empty
 5589:     -- bc_slice acts as an inner-join partner (same as filters).
 5590: 
 5591: =head2 aggregate
 5592: 
 5593:     â”€â”€â”€ AggregateSpec â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5594:     MEASURE ::= { fn : AGGFN; col : NAME?; as : ALIAS }
 5595:     AGGFN   ::= sum | avg | min | max | count | count_distinct
 5596:     ALIAS   ::= \w+
 5597:     SPEC    ::= { by : seq NAME; measures : seq MEASURE;
 5598:                   having : CRITERIA?;
 5599:                   sort_by : NAME Ã— {ASC,DESC}?; limit : â„•?; offset : â„•? }
 5600:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5601:     -- Pre-conditions (checked by _validate_aggregate_spec):
 5602:     âˆ€ m : measures â€¢
 5603:         m.fn âˆˆ {sum, avg, min, max, count, count_distinct}
 5604:         âˆ§ (m.fn â‰  count âˆ¨ m.fn = count_distinct âŸ¹ m.col â‰  âˆ…)
 5605:         âˆ§ m.as matches /^\w+\z/
 5606:     âˆ€ name : by â€¢ name âˆˆ dom col_db
 5607:     âˆ€ m : measures â€¢ m.col â‰  âˆ… âŸ¹ m.col âˆˆ dom col_db
 5608:     # aliases are unique and do not collide with 'by' names:
 5609:     âˆ€ i,j : 0 â€¥ #measures-1 â€¢ i â‰  j âŸ¹ measures(i).as â‰  measures(j).as
 5610:     âˆ€ m : measures; b : by â€¢ m.as â‰  b
 5611: 
 5612:     â”€â”€â”€ Aggregate â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5613:     ÎžDatabase_Join               -- state unchanged (Xi-transition)
 5614:     criteria?  : CRITERIA        -- pre-aggregation WHERE
 5615:     spec?      : SPEC
 5616:     result!    : seq GROUP_ROW
 5617:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5618:     -- GROUP_ROW has exactly the by-columns plus the measure aliases.
 5619:     -- Group key: tuple of by-column values from a merged row.
 5620:     group_key(row, by) == { b : by â€¢ b â†¦ row(b) }
 5621: 
 5622:     -- Fold function on the array backend:
 5623:     fold(rows, spec) ==
 5624:         let G = { k : IMAGE(group_key(r, by) | r : rows) â€¢ k â†¦ {} }
 5625:         for r : rows:
 5626:             G[group_key(r, by)] += r   -- accumulate per measure.fn
 5627:         for g : dom G:
 5628:             G[g] = { b : by â€¢ b â†¦ g(b) }
 5629:                  âˆª { m : measures â€¢ m.as â†¦ apply(m.fn, m.col, G[g].rows) }
 5630:         result_pre = ran G
 5631: 
 5632:     -- apply: maps aggregate function to its computed value.
 5633:     apply(count, âˆ…, rows)         == #rows
 5634:     apply(count_distinct, col, rows) == #{ r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 5635:     apply(sum, col, rows)         == Î£ { r : rows | is_num(r(col)) â€¢ r(col) }
 5636:     apply(avg, col, rows)         == sum(col, rows) / #{ r | is_num(r(col)) }
 5637:     apply(min, col, rows)         == min { r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 5638:     apply(max, col, rows)         == max { r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 5639: 
 5640:     -- Post-aggregation HAVING filter:
 5641:     result_having = [ g : result_pre | matches_having(g, spec.having) ]
 5642: 
 5643:     -- Grand total (by = []):
 5644:     #by = 0 âŸ¹ #result! = 1
 5645:     #by > 0 âˆ§ #result_having = 0 âŸ¹ result! = []
 5646: 
 5647:     -- Final result:
 5648:     result! = sort_and_page(result_having, spec)
 5649: 
 5650:     -- Backend dispatch:
 5651:     backend âˆˆ {sqlite, auto} âŸ¹ aggregation pushed to SQLite GROUP BY
 5652:     backend = array           âŸ¹ fold() applied in Perl
 5653: 
 5654:     -- Correctness invariant (both paths return identical rows):
 5655:     âˆ€ criteria?, spec? â€¢
 5656:         _sqlite_aggregate(criteria?, spec?) = _fold_aggregate(rows(criteria?), spec?)
 5657: 
 5658: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 5659: 
 5660:     selectall_arrayref : CRITERIA â†’ seq MERGED_ROW
 5661:     pre:  âˆ€ col : dom criteria â€¢ col âˆˆ dom self._col_db âˆª {self._join_col}
 5662:     post: result = _joined_query(criteria)
 5663:           result is sorted ascending by join_col value
 5664: 
 5665: =head2 selectall_array
 5666: 
 5667:     selectall_array : CRITERIA â†’ seq MERGED_ROW | MERGED_ROW?
 5668:     pre:  same as selectall_arrayref
 5669:     post: wantarray  => result = @{ selectall_arrayref(criteria) }
 5670:           !wantarray => result = selectall_arrayref(criteria)[0]  (or undef)
 5671: 
 5672: =head2 fetchrow_hashref
 5673: 
 5674:     fetchrow_hashref : CRITERIA â†’ MERGED_ROW?
 5675:     post: result = selectall_arrayref(criteria)[0]  (or undef if empty)
 5676: 
 5677: =head2 count
 5678: 
 5679:     count : CRITERIA â†’ â„•
 5680:     post: result = #selectall_arrayref(criteria)
 5681: 
 5682: =head2 columns
 5683: 
 5684:     columns : â†’ seq NAME
 5685:     post: result = sort(
 5686:               (â‹ƒ { i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ ran(dbs(i).columns) }
 5687:                \ dom removed_cols
 5688:                \ { local_jc(i) | i âˆˆ dom join_map âˆ§ local_jc(i) â‰  join_col })
 5689:           )
 5690: 
 5691: =head2 schema
 5692: 
 5693:     schema : â†’ NAME â‡¸ SCHEMA_INFO
 5694:     post: dom(result) = ran(columns())
 5695:           âˆ€ col : dom(result) â€¢
 5696:               result(col) = (last database containing col).schema()(col)
 5697: 
 5698: =head2 updated
 5699: 
 5700:     updated : â†’ â„•
 5701:     post: result = max { i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ dbs(i).updated() }
 5702: 
 5703: =head2 remove_column
 5704: 
 5705:     remove_column : NAME â†’ Database_Join
 5706:     pre:  col â‰  self._join_col
 5707:     post: self'._removed_cols = self._removed_cols âˆª {col}
 5708:           self'._col_db       = self._col_db \ {col}
 5709:           self'._col_cache    = undef
 5710:           self'._schema_cache = undef
 5711: 
 5712: =head2 AUTOLOAD
 5713: 
 5714:     AUTOLOAD : NAME Ã— CRITERIA â†’ VALUE | seq VALUE
 5715:     pre:  col âˆˆ dom self._col_db
 5716:           col does not begin with '_'
 5717:     post: let rows = _joined_query(criteria)
 5718:           wantarray  => result = { r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 5719:           !wantarray => result = rows(0)(col)  (or undef if rows is empty)
 5720: 
 5721: =head2 backend
 5722: 
 5723:     â”€â”€â”€ BackendDispatch â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5724:     backend        : {array, sqlite, auto}
 5725:     max_array_rows : â„•
 5726:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5727: 
 5728:     -- row_count(db): cheaply count rows in a component database.
 5729:     -- Uses dbi_source() COUNT(*) SQL for SQLite-backed sources,
 5730:     -- or the DA's own count() method when defined in its own package.
 5731:     -- Returns âŠ¥ (bottom / unknown) when neither is available.
 5732:     row_count(db) ==
 5733:         if (db has dbi_source() returning a SQLite dbh)
 5734:         then SELECT COUNT(*) FROM source_table
 5735:         else if (defined &{ref(db) ^ "::count"})
 5736:         then db.count()
 5737:         else âŠ¥
 5738: 
 5739:     -- Combined row count across all component databases.
 5740:     -- If any database returns âŠ¥, total is âŠ¥ (cannot determine).
 5741:     total_count ==
 5742:         if âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ row_count(dbs i) â‰  âŠ¥
 5743:         then Î£ { i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ row_count(dbs i) }
 5744:         else âŠ¥
 5745: 
 5746:     -- Dispatch rule for _joined_query:
 5747:     use_sqlite(C) ==
 5748:         backend = 'sqlite'
 5749:         âˆ¨ (backend = 'auto' âˆ§ total_count â‰  âŠ¥ âˆ§ total_count > max_array_rows)
 5750: 
 5751:     _joined_query(C) ==
 5752:         if use_sqlite(C)
 5753:         then _sqlite_join(C)
 5754:         else _joined_query_array(C)
 5755: 
 5756:     -- Result identity invariant: both paths return identical rows.
 5757:     âˆ€ C : CRITERIA â€¢
 5758:         _sqlite_join(C) = _joined_query_array(C)
 5759: 
 5760: =head1 STATE DIAGRAM
 5761: 
 5762: C<Database::Join> objects follow three independent finite state machines (FSMs).
 5763: Each FSM is described with an ASCII diagram showing valid states (boxes), the
 5764: triggers that cause transitions (arrows), and important side-effects.
 5765: 
 5766: =head2 FSM 1: Object Lifecycle
 5767: 
 5768: Governs the structural state of a C<Database::Join> instance.
 5769: Query methods (C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>,
 5770: C<count>, C<aggregate>, C<each_row>, C<columns>, C<schema>, C<updated>) are
 5771: schema-preserving (Xi-transitions) and are not shown because they do not change
 5772: state.  C<aggregate()> additionally triggers the SQLite cache lifecycle (FSM 2)
 5773: when the backend is C<'sqlite'> or C<'auto'>, just like any other query method.
 5774: 
 5775:     [pre-creation]
 5776:          |
 5777:          | new( databases => [...], join_column => '...' )
 5778:          |   Side-effect: _col_db routing table built;
 5779:          |                _autoload_pk cached from dbs[0]{id}
 5780:          v
 5781:     [CONSTRUCTED] <-----------------------------------------+
 5782:          |    |                                              |
 5783:          |    +--------------------------------------------+ |
 5784:          |    (query methods: no structural change)         | |
 5785:          |                                                  | |
 5786:          |-- remove_column( col ) -------> [COL_REMOVED] <--+ |
 5787:          |                                      |    |        |
 5788:          |   Side-effect: col removed from       |    |        |
 5789:          |   _col_db; _col_cache and             +----+        |
 5790:          |   _schema_cache cleared.              (idempotent;  |
 5791:          |   Join column cannot be removed.)      chainable)   |
 5792:          |                                                     |
 5793:          +-- add_database( db ) ----------> [DB_ADDED] <------+
 5794:                                                 |    |
 5795:              Side-effect: new columns added;    |    | add_database( db )
 5796:              _col_db extended; SQLite cache     |    | (chainable; each
 5797:              invalidated (if any).              +----+  extends the view)
 5798: 
 5799:     Note: COL_REMOVED and DB_ADDED are not mutually exclusive.
 5800:     Both transitions are legal on any valid object, in any order.
 5801: 
 5802:     Illegal triggers (always croak; object state is not changed):
 5803: 
 5804:       Trigger                              Error
 5805:       -----------------------------------  ---------------------------------
 5806:       new( databases => [] )               error_no_databases
 5807:       remove_column( join_column )         error_remove_join_column
 5808:       add_database( non-reference )        error_invalid_database
 5809:       new() with join_col absent from DB   error_join_col_absent
 5810: 
 5811: =head2 FSM 2: SQLite Cache Lifecycle
 5812: 
 5813: Governs the temporary SQLite cache used by the C<backend='sqlite'> and
 5814: C<backend='auto'> join paths.  The cache does not exist until the first
 5815: query on the SQLite path.
 5816: 
 5817:     [ABSENT] <----- add_database( db )
 5818:        |                  |
 5819:        |  (no temp file)  | Side-effect: old DBI handle disconnected;
 5820:        |                  |   _sqlite_cache deleted.
 5821:        |                  |
 5822:        |                  +<-------------------------------------------+
 5823:        |                                                               |
 5824:        | first query on SQLite path                                    |
 5825:        | Side-effect: File::Temp db created in tmpdir;                 |
 5826:        |   DBI connected; sources ATTACHed or spilled;                 |
 5827:        |   _sqlite_cache = { dbh, tmpfile, n, updated, ... }           |
 5828:        v                                                               |
 5829:     [FRESH] <--+                                                       |
 5830:        |        |                                                      |
 5831:        |        | subsequent queries                                   |
 5832:        |        | (cache reused; refaddr of _sqlite_cache unchanged)   |
 5833:        +--------+                                                      |
 5834:        |                                                               |
 5835:        | updated() timestamp of any source DA changes                  |
 5836:        | -- OR -- source row count changes                             |
 5837:        | Side-effect: none yet (_cache_fresh returns false)            |
 5838:        v                                                               |
 5839:     [STALE]                                                            |
 5840:        |                                                               |
 5841:        | next query                                                    |
 5842:        | Side-effect: old DBI handle disconnected; old temp file       |
 5843:        |   unlinked; new temp file built from current source data.     |
 5844:        +---------------------------------------------------------------+
 5845:        (transitions to FRESH)
 5846: 
 5847:     On object DESTROY:
 5848:       FRESH/STALE:  DBI handle disconnected; File::Temp object released
 5849:                     (temp file unlinked by File::Temp DESTROY).
 5850:       ABSENT:       No temp file exists; no-op.
 5851: 
 5852: =head2 FSM 3: Column Visibility (per column)
 5853: 
 5854: Each column in the logical view independently follows a two-state machine.
 5855: Transition from VISIBLE to REMOVED is one-way: C<add_database> never
 5856: restores a column that is in C<_removed_cols>.
 5857: 
 5858:     [VISIBLE] <-- initial state for every column at construction
 5859:          |    |
 5860:          |    | query / columns() / schema()
 5861:          |    | (column present in results; no state change)
 5862:          +----+
 5863:          |
 5864:          | remove_column( col )
 5865:          | Side-effect: col deleted from _col_db;
 5866:          |   _col_cache and _schema_cache cleared.
 5867:          v
 5868:     [REMOVED] <---+
 5869:          |         |
 5870:          |         | remove_column( col ) again
 5871:          |         | (idempotent; no error; no second side-effect)
 5872:          +---------+
 5873: 
 5874:     One-way invariant:
 5875:       If col is in _removed_cols, then add_database( db_that_has_col )
 5876:       does NOT re-add it.  Formal: col_db' = col_db âŠ• { c | c in
 5877:       ran(db.columns) \ {local_jc} \ removed }.
 5878: 
 5879:     Illegal trigger:
 5880:       remove_column( join_column )  -- error_remove_join_column (croaks;
 5881:                                        state unchanged)
 5882:
